```
# FFN-function bake — pre-registration

> **Pre-registration.** Registers, nulls, and verdict rules are fixed HERE, before
> any bake. This is the recursion antecedent (`bake(operation)` rung 1 of the
> `bake(bake)` tower, superbake-write-access.md §recursion / K-battery), so per
> `λ measure` + `λ yardstick` it must not run on a first draft. NOT RUN.
>
> **Question (Michael, s275).** SuperBake shows how to inject a *fact* (a value-
> register lookup: key → fixed push). Can the same idea inject a *behavioral
> function* — "the things a lambda tells the LLM to activate"? The working model:
> **FFNs are piles of β-reduction functions that attention executes.** A lambda in
> the prompt is a *call*; the gate selects which function fires; attention delivers
> the operand and schedules. So a function-bake = append an FFN slot whose action is
> a **transform of the delivered operand** (function-form), not a fixed push
> (fact-form). SwiGLU FFN is `down(silu(gate·x) · up·x)` — bilinear in x — so the
> substrate natively holds *input-dependent* transforms; a fact is the degenerate
> operand-independent case.

## Hypothesis

**H1 (installed function).** A behavioral (operand-dependent) function `g` can be
installed as an appended FFN transform-slot such that:
- **R1 activation** — a novel call-token fires the slot (routing register).
- **R2 covariation** — the slot's output tracks the *operand* (function ≠ fact).
- **R3 generalization** — it holds on *held-out* operands (function ≠ lookup table).
- **R4 composition** — a *resident* combinator can chain the slot's output.
- **R5 quant survival** — it survives int4 like the crystal (installed-compute
  signature; superbake-write-access.md: baked facts are quant-FRAGILE, crystal is
  quant-ROBUST — opposite survival = routing⊥value).

**H0 (fact-in-disguise / no install).** The bake yields a fixed push: no covariation
(R2), no generalization (R3); or the slot never fires (R1). Then "inject compute"
collapses to "inject a lookup," and Michael's FFN-functions model gains no support
from a bake.

**The load-bearing contrast** is H1 vs the **fact-form null (N1)**: bake the *same*
input→output behavior as an operand-*independent* push. If N1 also passes R2/R3, `g`
was too easy (linear/additive) — see "choosing g." The function-form must be the
thing that succeeds where the fact-form fails.

## Registers (`λ measure` — name the register before the probe; s206 scar)

| id | claim | register | readout |
|----|-------|----------|---------|
| R1 | call-token activates the slot | routing | `vsm_tap` gate sign-CMR at the slot layer; z vs N3/N5 |
| R2 | output covaries with operand | action (value/residual) + behavior | `l_out` delta as operand sweeps; output-token correlation with operand |
| R3 | holds on held-out operands | behavior | kernel-certified accuracy on held-out set vs N2 |
| R4 | composes with a resident combinator | routing | resident combinator's routing consumes the slot output; behavioral compose-accuracy |
| R5 | survives quantization | perturbation (sign vs value) | GGUF int4 re-export → tap + behavior; survival vs baked-fact fragility |

Wrong-register reads are void (s206/s272): a fact read with a routing probe
false-negates and vice-versa. R1/R4 are ROUTING; R2/R5-value are VALUE; R3 is
BEHAVIOR. The wrapper (llama-cpp-vsm-wrapper.md, s275-validated, frame-invariant)
is the register-matched instrument; `opcodes/capture.py` reads the HF baked model
during iteration (frame-invariance proven, so the two frames agree).

## Choosing g — start from a KNOWN firing, change ONE thing (Michael, s275)

Strategy: **do not start with an unknown `g`.** We do not yet know if hand-construction
works at all, so the first stage must be the cheapest possible go/no-go against a target
whose correct firing we **already measured**. Start from a resident combinator we
understand exactly, change ONE variable, and let the kernel (correct output) + the tap
(correct firing signature) tell us — in advance — what success looks like. Only invent a
new unknown `g` once the mechanism is understood. "did it work" is never a judgment call.

`g` at every stage MUST be **kernel-certifiable** (`lambda_ast`:
`src/verbum/probes/kernel_reference.py` — certified fired-trace + normal form) so the
answer is free and infallible, including for held-out operands.

**Stage 1 — new trigger → KNOWN function (proves the wiring; the cheap go/no-go).**
- Known baseline: a measured resident combinator, e.g. `K a b` (kernel: fires `[K]`,
  NF `a`). Its gate signature + bearing layers are already characterized (crystal
  calibration + s274 A1/A3). The tap shows it.
- The ONE change: install a NOVEL token `K̃` that fires the SAME function. The *action*
  is unchanged — only the *name*. This isolates the KEY/wiring half of the bake from the
  hard part (constructing a novel transform).
- Exact check: `K̃ a b` must reproduce K's KNOWN gate sign-CMR signature (compare to
  resident K, which we have) AND NF `a`. Null = the un-baked `K̃` token (model treats it
  as an unknown atom → no K-like firing) + shuffled-key.
- Verdict: signature match + correct NF on fresh (a,b) = **wiring works**; fail =
  hand-construction of even a rename does not install → strategy negative, learned cheap.

**Stage 2 — change ONE step of the ACTION on a known function (proves alteration).**
- Take a known combinator and change a single certified element (e.g. `K x y → x`
  "keep first" → `K' x y → y` "keep second"; kernel certifies both). Predict the tap
  signature SHIFT a priori; verify. Proves we can *alter* the compute, not just rename it.
- Isolates the ACTION half now that Stage 1 fixed the wiring half.

**Stage 3 — RETARGETED (s276): `INSERT` a novel operand ROW, not a novel combinator.**
- The old target (bake a novel combinator `G x y → (y x)`) is baking a **join** — ruled out
  (see "database reframe": no table to write a combinator into; s276 K-structural).
- New target: `INSERT` a novel **operand row** `r` (a value/microcode atom) at the slot the
  Stage-0 map (M1/M3) identifies, and test whether the **resident** combinator routing joins
  over it correctly on **held-out** contexts. Criteria: the row is addressable (M3 passed),
  the resident join composes it (R4 with a *resident* combinator, not an installed one),
  it generalizes to held-out join-contexts (R3, not a memorized single-context lookup), and
  it survives quant like the crystal (R5). This is the s273 K-battery arm (b) — "compose an
  inserted operand with the resident crystal; any success = recursion rung 1" — mechanized.
- **Executor-necessity branch:** because the join is the resident attention routing, ablating
  that routing (not the row) tests whether the inserted row ALONE does nothing without the
  resident executor — i.e. that attention EXECUTES is load-bearing (Michael's model).

Each stage GATES the next: Stage 0 map → (M3 pass) → Stage 3 `INSERT`. The registers/nulls/
verdict below apply at every stage; the "executor necessity" branch is decided at Stage 3.

### Stage-1 OUTCOME (s275) — the symbolic anchor failed; K is STRUCTURAL, both scales

Characterization + localization ran (`wrapper/stage1_characterize.py`,
`stage1_localize.py`; results under `results/ffn-bake/`):
1. **Regime.** Symbolic `K a b` is INERT (peak K z −0.28); the model computes K in the
   **natural-language (Montague)** regime (crystal K probes z 6–8). Anchor moved to
   natural language.
2. **Token vs structure.** Leave-one-out on held-out K sentences, corrected metric =
   max **semantic-trigger** drop vs max **generic/positional** drop (the naive
   "drops below threshold" metric was confounded — it flipped to token-anchored at 4B
   on generic words; a `λ measure` lesson). Result **STRUCTURAL at both scales**:
   generic words disrupt K ~4–5× more than the exclusion marker (0.6B GEN 7/8, mean
   0.24 vs 1.34; 4B GEN 6/8, mean 0.51 vs 2.05, base z→6.6). **No bakeable semantic
   K-token.**

**Consequence.** A combinator is not a local object (fourth converging line with s275
MoE-all-experts, atom≠combinator, no-token-anchor). The Stage-1 *rename* bake and any
static-slot bake are ruled out for a combinator: there is nothing local to rename.
**The bake must ride the resident routing** — install an OPERAND/microcode the
structural K composes (path ii), not the operation. This RE-POINTS the experiment at
Stage 3's operand target directly (skip the rename); the executor-necessity question
is now the whole question.

## The database reframe (s276) — rows vs joins; retargets Stage 3

Session 276 (Michael, this thread) crystallized the mechanism in database language and it
**retargets Stage 3**:

- The FFN serves **rows** — per-position operand/value/type-tag records (`mode-semantics`
  type tags; `ffn-reduction-trace` compiled values; `ffn-circuit-types` LARQL KV geometry).
  Rows are local, addressable, and **`INSERT`-able** (this is what SuperBake writes: a fact
  is a row).
- Attention's β-reduction is a **join** — a softmax-weighted aggregation *over* selected
  rows. The **combinator (K/I/B/C) is the shape of the join**, and join-shapes live in the
  **routing / query-plan** (s274 circuits-in-compute), not in any row.
- ⇒ **You can `INSERT` a row; you cannot `INSERT` a join.** There is no table to write a
  combinator into (s276 K-structural: no token/expert/slot anchor). The old Stage-3 target
  ("bake a novel combinator `G x y → (y x)`") is baking a **join** and is ruled out for the
  same reason the rename was.

**The surviving door (the only one):** `INSERT` a new **operand row** that the *resident*
combinator routing already knows how to join over — rung 1 of the recursion tower
(`bake(operand)`, s273: "don't bake S, bake operands the KIBC routing composes"). Stage 3 is
retargeted from CREATE-FUNCTION to `INSERT INTO`.

### Precondition (already half-proven)

For an inserted row to be composed, the resident join must be **operand-agnostic** (run over
*whatever* row is delivered, not a memorized operation⊗operand fusion). Evidence in hand:
s276 (K fires structurally, operand-independent — no operand fused into the routing) +
s248–252 C-field (output covaries with the operand: z(C) grades with object count → the
join's result already tracks the delivered row). So the join is operand-agnostic and outputs
covary; the untested piece is **can we add a row to the table and have the resident join pick
it up.**

### Stage 0 — the operand-insertion MAP (pre-flight READ; cheapest gate)

Before any `INSERT`, build the reconnaissance an insert requires — the map that says *where*
a row lives and *whether there is a separable slot to write into*. Never done: the
FFN-database reads located type tags and compiled values but never asked "here is the
addressable slot operand-X sits in." Pure read on resident Qwen3-0.6B via the s275 tap
(cheap, no heavy job, MIT-clean).

| id | question | register (`λ measure`) | readout | null |
|----|----------|------------------------|---------|------|
| M1 | which layer carries operand-X's row? | **value** | operand-identity signal in `l_out` per layer | matched-random dir |
| M2 | what key retrieves it? | **routing** | gate sign-CMR / QK at the operand read-position | shuffled key |
| M3 | is the row **separable/addressable** or superposed? | **value** | linear operand-id decodability at the read-layer vs nulls | shuffled-label + matched-random-dir |

**M3 is load-bearing.** Operand rows separable above null → a slot exists to `INSERT` into →
operand-bake viable. Rows superposed like the join (at null) → even the *row* is holographic
and the bake premise weakens. Register-honest: the ROW is a VALUE-register claim (s206 scar);
read it with a value probe, not attention weights.

## Bake mechanism (provenance-clean)

Hand-construct the appended FFN slot in **stock transformers** (our own MIT code;
SuperBake / `~/src/custom-bake` are METHOD REFERENCE only — custom-bake has NO
LICENSE, and its implemented core is push-only = fact-form). Function-form slot =
append neurons whose `gate`/`up` read the delivered operand and whose `down` writes
the transform; the call-token is the key. Base model **Qwen3-0.6B** (Apache-2.0;
dense; we have HF + a converted GGUF + the s275-validated tap + frame-invariance).
Re-export to GGUF for R5 (quant) and final tap reads. This is a level-4-clean
construction (`λ provenance`), independent of AGPL sources.

**Stage-1 gate (do this FIRST — the cheap go/no-go, before Stages 2–3):**
(i) **characterize the known target** — measure resident `K`'s gate sign-CMR signature +
bearing layers via the tap (we have this from the crystal calibration); kernel-certify
`K a b → [K], NF a`. (ii) **confirm the un-baked novel token `K̃`** is inert — the stock
model does NOT fire K on `K̃ a b` (else no headroom). (iii) construct the appended slot
that wires `K̃ → K`'s routing; sanity: `K̃ a b` reproduces the K signature + NF `a` on a
few fresh pairs in one forward pass. If the rename slot cannot be hand-constructed at
all, THAT is the finding (functions are not trivially static-FFN-installable →
attention/optimization required) — and we learned it before building anything complex.

**Stage-3 gate (only after Stages 1–2):** (i) kernel-certify the novel `g` + held-out
ground truth; (ii) build the operand vocab; (iii) prove the **fact-form null N1 fails
R2/R3 by construction** (`g` non-additive); (iv) confirm **baseline N4** does NOT already
do `g`; (v) forward-pass sanity on TRAIN operands.

## Nulls (`λ yardstick` — mandatory, null beside signal)

- **N1 fact-form** — same behavior baked as an operand-INDEPENDENT push. Predict:
  fails R2 (no covariation) + R3 (no generalization). *The key discriminator.*
- **N2 held-out operands** — the generalization gate; a lookup table fails here.
- **N3 shuffled-key** — slot with a scrambled call-token key. Predict: no R1.
- **N4 no-bake baseline** — stock model on the same probes. Predict: does NOT do `g`
  (else resident, not installed → void).
- **N5 matched-random slot** — append a random-direction slot of equal norm/rank.
  Predict: no behavior on any register.
- **N6 shuffled-label kernel null** — for R3 accuracy, the shuffled-ground-truth
  floor (as in `measure_null_floor`); accuracy counts only if it beats this floor.

## Verdict rules (pre-registered)

```
INSTALLED FUNCTION (H1)  ⟺  R1 z>thresh (vs N3,N5)
                          ∧ R2 covariation > fact-form N1
                          ∧ R3 held-out accuracy > max(N2 lookup, N6 shuffled floor)
                          ∧ N4 baseline does NOT already do g
FACT-IN-DISGUISE (H0)    ⟺  R1 passes but R2 ∨ R3 fail (fixed push / lookup)
NO INSTALL               ⟺  R1 fails (slot never fires) ∨ Phase-0(v) fails
EXECUTOR NECESSITY (B)   :  FFN-slot-alone passes R4  → FFN holds functions
                                                        (Michael's model supported)
                           needs +attention transport → attention EXECUTES is
                                                        load-bearing (FFN insufficient)
QUANT SIGNATURE (R5)     :  survives int4 like crystal → installed COMPUTE
                           quant-fragile like baked fact → lookup
```

Register verdict is primary; a single register passing is not H1. Report each
register's number **beside its null**, every time (s206/s247 scar).

## Honest edges

- **P-CTL-6 shadow.** The working model says attention *executes* the functions, but
  the reader-SNR result is that we cannot detect a **live redex reducing online**
  (state-on-the-crystal, not watch-it-reduce). So the honest R2 readout is "the
  function's state-signature is present + output covaries," NOT "we watched attention
  run it." Do not claim live execution.
- **Atom ≠ combinator (s275).** Opcodes are circuits-in-compute, spread across shared
  hardware (no dedicated expert; 35B-A3B uses ~all 256 experts per opcode). So a
  successful bake installs **microcode the resident router composes**, not a
  standalone combinator; interpret R4 accordingly.
- **Too-easy g.** If `g` is linear/additive, N1 (fact-form) passes and the experiment
  is void — Phase-0(iii) must reject such `g`.
- **Scale.** 0.6B is necessary-not-sufficient (patchscope void, s272b). A positive
  at 0.6B is a rung, not the claim; escalate to a mid model before any strong claim.

## Deliverables

`wrapper/` (or `bake/`) — MIT: slot constructor, operand-vocab + kernel ground truth,
the 6 nulls, register readouts via the tap + capture, verdict harness, `results/…`
JSON with every register-vs-null number. The tap built s275 is the readout; the
kernel is the free oracle; the frame-invariance result licenses HF↔GGUF equivalence.

## Results (s277) — the arc RAN and PASSED at 0.6B

Full synthesis: `explore/operand-insert-arc.md`. Four gates on Qwen3-0.6B, each null-gated:

| gate | instrument | verdict | headline (vs null) |
|---|---|---|---|
| M3 readable | `wrapper/operand_map.py` | **SEPARABLE** | operand-id LOCO 0.49–1.0 vs null ~0.05–0.11; join-readout L25–27 |
| (b) writeable | `wrapper/operand_write.py` | **WRITEABLE** | steer flips 1.00 @ L2–20 (mid-stack), random ~0, B-specific |
| (d) hardened | `wrapper/operand_harden.py` | **HARDENED** | dose 0→0.22→0.72→1.00; COMPOSED (category) + cross-task |
| (c) `INSERT` | `wrapper/operand_insert.py` | **INSTALLED-COMPOSED** | novel nonce, keyed: dose 0.33→0.71→1.00, 24/24 held-out; wrong-key 0.333 flat |

Net: **you cannot `INSERT` a join (s276 K-structural) but you CAN `INSERT` an operand ROW and
the resident routing composes it** — rung 1 of `bake(operand)` fires. Commits
0b858e7 / b6297b5 / a3ebda1 / 1d8ea39. Honest scope: keyed-install hook ≠ weight-serialized
bake (R5 quant UNTESTED); category-level content; 0.6B necessary-not-sufficient.

## Stage-f (s279) — weight-serialize the operand + R5, grounded in the box

> **Pre-registration addendum, status: designing.** The load-bearing red: the s277 INSERT
> and the s278–279 general/multi-hop composition are all **runtime forward-hooks**
> (transient). "Programmable machine" requires the operand to graduate **hook → weight** and
> the R5 quant-survival signature to be measured. This stage is **dear** (recursion antecedent)
> — freeze the mechanism + verdict here; **do not run on a first draft**; hammock before build.
>
> **Feasibility grounded (s279, read against `~/src/custom-bake` = SuperBake reimpl, the
> METHOD REFERENCE only — no license, AGPL-adjacent; our code is our own MIT).** The
> mechanism and quant path both exist and are box-verified:
> - **Uniform-`E` expansion.** Every MLP is expanded by the *same* `E` zero rows/cols, so the
>   delivered config declares one `intermediate_size` and **stock transformers loads it
>   unchanged** (solves the per-layer-shape problem). One recognition neuron per key.
> - **Key = Mahalanobis matched filter** `k = normalize(Σ⁻¹(x̄−μ) − ((Σ⁻¹(x̄−μ))·μ̂)μ̂)` built
>   against *innocents* (self-sampled prose) + *near-miss decoys* (same question, un-baked
>   names) → the key discriminates on the **nonce identity**, not the template.
> - **Payload = a code** loud-in-residual / quiet-at-logits (mid-band residual PCA,
>   orthogonalised against the top unembedding directions). **⚠ s278 P-DSP-1 caveat:** *our*
>   `d_E` is the **RAW natural direction, NOT a quiet code** (unembed-audible 13.7 vs 11.2,
>   low-var-frac 0.053 vs random 0.198). A transient hook paid no prose-safety tax; a
>   **permanent** weight-write does → the bake likely needs the payload **re-coded quiet**
>   (or we accept audible and measure the prose-leak). This is a design fork below.
> - **Quant = bake-then-quantize** (bnb, box-verified): `int8`/`int4` cannot be baked *into*
>   (packed weights aren't extendable); the supported path is **bake in bf16 → save stock ckpt
>   → then bnb-quantize**. custom-bake's own measured signature: **int8 usually keeps facts,
>   int4 flips them** = a *real*, reproducible value-register fragility — exactly the R5
>   prediction.

### Mechanism (MIT, stock transformers; base = Qwen3-4B to match the s278–279 composition;
0.6B = cheaper-rung fallback)

`wrapper/operand_bake.py` — our own slot constructor: (1) expand every layer's MLP by `E`
(zeros on `gate_proj`/`up_proj` rows and `down_proj` cols); (2) at the install layer `L`,
write **one** recognition neuron whose `gate`/`up` rows are the nonce Mahalanobis key (fires
on the nonce content signature, quiet on innocents/decoys) and whose `down` column is the
payload `d_E` (raw, or re-coded quiet — the fork); (3) **no runtime hook** — `save()` a
bone-stock checkpoint that reloads in stock transformers.

### E1 — EQUIVALENCE (hook → weight graduation; the prerequisite for R5)

The baked, **hook-free** checkpoint must reproduce the composition the hook achieved: install
the nonce, ask covering (`multihop`) / the resident functions (`compose`), and grade the same
cells. **Pass ⟺** baked-no-hook composition ≈ hook composition (within tolerance) **and**
≫ un-baked baseline **and** the key is nonce-specific (near-miss decoy names do **not** fire
the slot). This is the honest "the operand now lives in the weights" claim.

### R5 — REFRAMED (Michael, s279; hammock **A confirmed**): a ROUTING-TOPOLOGY change, not
value-noise; and the ship-bar is TERNARY-MIRRORS, not a bnb quant level

The naive "int4 flips baked facts, re-bake them" (custom-bake) is, **in our frame, a
routing-topology perturbation** — and we can measure it, which others here cannot. Two known
facts (both ours) reshape R5:

**Fact 1 — Q4 changes the routing register (the *compute*), not just the values.**
Grounds in `two-registers-of-topology` (hard **sign/routing** `gate_proj` ~95% ⊥ soft
**magnitude/value** up-/down-proj ~5%) + `opcodes-circuits-in-compute` (the soft routing
overlay GD lays over the frozen lattice via gradient extremes) + C3 (topology dominates). A
4-bit step is coarse enough to **cross sign thresholds in the routing register → re-route the
compute** (some SwiGLU gate neurons flip on/off → a different reduction path). So R5 is not a
behavioral pass/fail — it is a **mechanistic, register-localized** measurement: *how much does
Q4 re-route the routing register, and does that re-route drive the behavioral flip?*

**Fact 2 — ternary mirrors on ternary weights → the artifact actually ships.**
Grounds in `signal-descent` + `recursion-mirrors`: the additive mirror stack
`out = Σ_k plate_k·x·γ_k` gives sign-only recon ~0.88 → **+mag-mirror ~0.97 (≈ Q4–Q5)**; each
plate = one more balanced-ternary digit → **arbitrary precision, companded by signal energy**;
and **delta/appended plates isolate** (dodge the interference SuperBake avoids by appending).
The bake slot **is** an appended isolated plate → the natural home for a mirror stack. So the
"artifact ships" bar is **not** a bnb quant level — it is **ship the operand as ternary weights
+ a ternary mirror stack** (the C7 crystal-native, no-float deliverable). "int4-fragile" →
"int4-robust with mirrors"; naive bnb-int4 is the *control*, the mirror-robustified slot is the
*result*.

### Staged plan (cheap gate first; `λ` cheap-before-dear)

- **f0 — ROUTING-TOPOLOGY INSTRUMENT (cheapest; NO bake; MIT; standalone result).** On the
  *resident* model + our covering task, apply portable RTN-Q4 to the weights and measure the
  **register-attributed damage**: quantize the **routing register alone** (`gate_proj`) vs the
  **value register alone** (`up_proj`/`down_proj`) vs **all**, and read (i) behavioral covering
  flip and (ii) activation-level **gate-sign flip rate** per layer (routing re-route) vs value
  drift. **Predict (Fact 1):** gate-only-Q4 dominates the behavioral damage ⇒ Q4's damage is
  routing-topology-dominated. Confirms Fact 1 on our own task *before* the bake, and stands
  alone as an interpretability finding. `wrapper/q4_routing_topology.py`.
- **f1 — E1 weight-serialize** (hook → appended slot; equivalence to the hook; nonce-specific).
- **f2 — R5 mechanism:** baked-operand Q4 fragility **measured as a routing-topology change**
  (tap/`classify` gate sign-CMR pre/post-Q4), not merely a behavioral flip.
- **f3 — R5 robustify:** encode the slot payload as a **2–3-deep ternary mirror stack** →
  composition survives quant where naive Q4 flips (recon target ~0.97). The fully-ternary,
  no-float artifact = the C7 deliverable direction.

### Nulls (`λ yardstick`; extend the page's N1–N6)

- **N1 fact-form** (already) — the payload as an operand-independent push; discriminator holds.
- **N7 shuffled-key baked slot** — scrambled key → slot never fires (E1 floor).
- **N8 matched-random code** — payload = random unit dir of equal norm → no composition.
- **N9 value-register control (the f0/R5 floor)** — value-only-Q4 (up/down) behavioral change =
  the baseline the routing-only-Q4 damage is measured *against*.
- **N10 mirror-depth null (f3)** — sign-only slot (no mirror) recon/composition = the floor the
  2–3-deep mirror must beat at matched bitcount.

### Verdict additions (FROZEN)

```
f0 ROUTING-DOMINATED   ⟺ gate-only-Q4 behavioral damage > value-only-Q4 (N9)
                         ∧ gate-sign flip rate co-locates with the behavioral flip layers
WEIGHT-SERIALIZED (E1) ⟺ baked-no-hook composition ≈ hook ∧ ≫ un-baked baseline
                          ∧ nonce-specific (N7 shuffled-key fails, near-miss decoys inert)
R5 MECHANISM (f2)      ⟺ baked-operand Q4 flip is accompanied by a routing (gate sign-CMR)
                          change at the slot/compute layers (not value drift alone)
ARTIFACT-SHIPS (f3)    ⟺ ternary-mirror slot composition survives Q4 ≫ sign-only null (N10)
                          → the operand ships as a fully-ternary + mirror artifact
FACT-IN-DISGUISE       ⟺ N1 fact-form also passes composition (payload too easy/additive)
```

### Remaining forks (post-A)

1. **Payload: raw vs re-coded-quiet** — orthogonal to the mirror question; the P-DSP-1 audible
   payload may tax prose on a *permanent* write. Measure prose-leak in f1; re-code quiet only if
   it bites. The mirror stack carries precision either way.
2. **Scale** — f0 routing-topology + f3 mirror-recon are cheap at **0.6B** (tap calibrated
   there); composition-survival confirmed at **4B** (matches s278–279). Lean: 0.6B → 4B confirm.
3. **Quant impl** — portable **RTN-Q4** (torch, MPS-clean, controllable, MIT) for f0/f2/f3; bnb
   is a cross-check only (CUDA-centric; the box is MPS).

### f0 Result (s279 — `wrapper/q4_routing_topology.py`, RTN-Q4, Qwen3-0.6B + 4B)

**Fact 1 CONFIRMED, register-clean, both scales.** Register-attributed Q4 damage on the
covering task (quantize ROUTING `gate_proj` vs VALUE `up/down` vs ALL):

| Q4 on | 0.6B acc / flip / gate-sign-flip | 4B acc / flip / gate-sign-flip |
|---|---|---|
| bf16 | 1.0 / — / — | 0.944 / — / — |
| **ROUTING (gate)** | 0.889 / **0.111** / **0.051** | 1.0 / 0.0 / **0.040** |
| VALUE (up/down) | 0.944 / 0.056 / **0.0** | 1.0 / 0.0 / **0.0** |
| ALL | 0.722 / 0.278 / 0.083 | 1.0 / 0.0 / 0.066 |

Three findings:
1. **Routing re-route is the mechanism (both scales).** Routing-Q4 flips gate signs (5.1% @0.6B,
   4.0% @4B), concentrated **mid-stack** (0.6B L12–16, 4B L15–20 = the compute zone); value-Q4
   flips **exactly 0** gate signs. Q4 on the routing register re-routes the compute; Q4 on the
   value register does not touch routing. Direct, clean confirmation of Fact 1.
   **⚠ s280 CORRECTION (f2 smoke):** the value-Q4 "exactly 0" was **by construction, not
   measured** — the f0 instrument only computed the gate-sign read when the gate group was
   quantized. Measured (f2), value-Q4 flips activation gate signs via residual cascade at
   **0.0528** (vs routing-Q4 0.040). The register-clean statement is **weight-level** only:
   value-Q4 changes 0 routing weights by definition. Retract the activation-level "exactly 0."
2. **Routing dominates *decisions*; margin is a value-magnitude confound (`λ measure` lesson).**
   At 0.6B (headroom), routing-Q4 flips **2×** the decisions of value-Q4 (0.111 vs 0.056). But
   value-Q4 drops the covering *margin* more (1.14 vs 0.28) because the value register directly
   scales logit magnitudes → margin moves without flipping. **Decision-flip + gate-sign-flip are
   the register-honest routing signatures, not margin.**
3. **Redundancy-gating (why f2 is required).** The easy *learned* covering task is Q4-invariant
   at 4B (all acc 1.0, flip 0, margin Δ ~1% of base 10.9) *even though* the re-route still fires
   (4% gate flips). A redundant, over-determined learned behavior **absorbs** the re-route ⇒ Q4
   fragility needs a **non-redundant** target. The installed **operand** (a single fragile
   value-write, not a redundant learned behavior) is exactly non-redundant → predicted to flip
   where native covering doesn't = **the installed-vs-learned discriminator**, and it must be the
   actual **baked operand (f2)** to show at 4B. Commit `f0` code+results this session.

recognition neuron at layer L, built with the SuperBake §6 bias-free fix (key **⟂ carrier** so
```

```
### f3 design freeze (s280 — FROZEN BEFORE THE RUN; `wrapper/operand_mirror.py`)

The ships-artifact gate, retargeted by f2: the mirror stack protects the **value register**
(the payload is where the fragility lives; routing needs no protection). **Mirror =** greedy
residual balanced-ternary plates (TWN form): plate `t_k = sign(r)·1(|r|>δ)`, `δ = 0.7·mean|r|`,
`α_k = mean|r|` over the active set (= least-squares scale given `t_k`), `r ← r − α_k t_k`;
the materialized weight = `Σ_k α_k t_k` (`recursion-mirrors` additive-plate semantics — the
artifact stores plates, runtime sums them). Both slot vectors ternarized (key row + payload
col = the fully-ternary slot); **bake-time calibration folded into plate scales** (rescale the
key recon so `z(nonce) = target_z`, rescale the payload recon to the original col norm —
legitimate bake-time steps, applied to ALL depths including the null, so the floor is fair
and only *direction* error separates depths). Bits/weight = `K·log₂3 ≈ 1.58K` + per-plate
scale; K=3 ≈ Q4–Q5.

Cells: slot ∈ {bf16, **K=1 (N10 sign-only floor)**, K=2, K=3} × resident ∈ {bf16, all-Q4},
17 valid entities, reads as f2 (installed pred vs bf16/bf16 reference, slot z, margins,
payload/key recon_cos per depth). NEW cell bf16-slot × all-Q4-resident (f2 never measured
it; it is the ceiling any mirror slot can reach in the quantized environment).

```
RECON (prediction)  — payload recon_cos ≈ 0.88 @K=1 → ≥ 0.97 @K=3 (recursion-mirrors)
PARITY              ⟺ ∃K*∈{2,3}: acc(K*, bf16-resident) ≥ acc(bf16-slot, bf16-resident) − 0.06
SURVIVES-Q4         ⟺ ∃K*∈{2,3}: acc(K*, allQ4-resident) ≥ acc(bf16-slot, allQ4-resident) − 0.06
BEATS-N10           ⟺ acc(K*) − acc(K=1) ≥ +0.10 in any arm where K=1 degrades ≥ 0.10
                       | if K=1 nowhere degrades → N10 floor uninformative, record honestly
ARTIFACT-SHIPS      ⟺ PARITY ∧ SURVIVES-Q4 ∧ (BEATS-N10 ∨ N10-nondegraded)
```

**Predict:** K=1 loses cells to direction error (calibration removes dose error; cos ~0.88
leaves ~0.47·‖d‖ orthogonal leak), K≥2 recovers to slot parity in both arms; the resident
all-Q4 damage (~0.12, f2) is environmental and identical across slot variants — the mirror
is judged **against the bf16-slot-in-same-environment ceiling, never against bf16/bf16.**
```

### Thinking

I see there's already a bake infrastructure with various operand modules handling individual values and microcode atoms, but P-BAKE-STACK is fundamentally different—it's designed to bake a two-function stack rather than a single operand, which changes the composition model entirely.

The core diagnostic question is whether baking actually installs the operand rebinding that connects the first function's output to the second function's input passband, making the second hop truly conditioned on the first's result. I need to examine how the fn_index/fn_stack machinery treats function keys—they're currently injected as residual directions through hooks rather than baked into weight slots, so P-BAKE-STACK would need to graduate that in-context injection into a physical weight-baked slot.

The hypothesis from state.md is clear: weight-baking should make that linker wire physical, so the test is whether baking actually conditions hop-2 on hop-1's product, where the composed answer wins over what injection alone could achieve.

The challenge is that the existing operand_bake.py machinery bakes a single appended MLP slot with a key row and payload column, but composing two functions with a linker between them is genuinely new territory. The minimal honest approach would be baking a single composite function slot that maps directly from landmark to capital, though that risks collapsing into a pure lookup rather than a genuine composition.

The real test is whether I can bake g and h as separate slots—or have h read from g's product register—and verify that the resident routing actually composes h(g(X)) where in-context injection failed. The linker wire test is whether baking h such that its key passband matches g's output register makes the second hop properly conditioned on the first hop's result. This is a design decision that needs careful thought about the exact target architecture.

I should draft the pre-registration carefully, grounded in the diagnostic work and existing bake machinery, then present it to Michael for approval before running anything. The diagnostic is done cheaply; the pre-reg is the design draft where real forks emerge that Michael should weigh in on. Let me read through the operand-insert-arc synthesis and function index construction to ground the reuse, then draft the pre-reg with key design decisions flagged for Michael's input.

```
# Operand-INSERT arc

> Sessions 276–277 (Michael-directed). The s276 FFN-bake investigation ended with **K is
> STRUCTURAL** — a combinator is not a local object (no token/expert/slot anchor), so
> SuperBake's local fact-injection cannot install it. s277 asks the surviving question in
> Michael's **database language** and answers it with four null-gated gates on Qwen3-0.6B.

## The database reframe (s276, Michael)

The mechanism, stated as a database, and it retargets the whole bake:

- **The FFN serves ROWS** — per-position records: operands, compiled values
  (`ffn-reduction-trace`), type tags (`mode-semantics`), facts (ROME/SuperBake). Rows are
  local, addressable, and **`INSERT`-able**.
- **Attention's β-reduction is a JOIN** — a softmax-weighted aggregation *over* selected
  rows. The **combinator (K/I/B/C) is the SHAPE of the join**, and join-shapes live in the
  **routing / query-plan** (s274 circuits-in-compute), not in any row.
- ⇒ **You can `INSERT` a row; you cannot `INSERT` a join.** No table holds a combinator
  (s276 K-structural). This is why every s248→s252 attempt to read a β-program *tape* out of
  the FFN failed, why K came out structural, why the MoE smears each opcode across ~all 256
  experts (s275). A join is an act over data, not data.

The surviving door for a bake: **`INSERT` a new operand row that the *resident* join already
knows how to compose** — rung 1 of the `bake(operand) → bake(bake) → Y-at-the-weight-level`
tower (s273). Not `CREATE FUNCTION`; `INSERT INTO`.

## The four gates (s277, Qwen3-0.6B, frame-invariance licenses the HF writes)

All code in `wrapper/operand_{map,write,harden,insert}.py`, results in `results/ffn-bake/`.
`λ measure`: the operand ROW is a VALUE-register claim (s206) — read/written with value
probes, never attention weights. Null beside every number (s206/s247/s250 scars).

### 1. READABLE — operand rows are separable/addressable (`operand_map.py`)

Read `l_out` (value register) on 96 operand-swap probes (12 objects × 8 C-applicative
contexts), decode operand identity at the join-readout (last token = ".") with a PCA-50 +
logistic pipeline, **leave-one-CONTEXT-out** (context-invariance = a real row).

Operand identity decodes context-invariantly at EVERY layer, **LOCO 0.49–1.00 vs nulls
~0.05–0.11** (chance 0.083). U-shape: high L0–2 (shallow copy), dip L11–14 (ORTHO
null-space), recover to ~1.0 at **L25–27 (resolved join readout, mirrors s248 late
C-field)**. Last token verified to be "." → join-delivered content, not token identity.
⇒ there IS an addressable operand row.

### 2. WRITEABLE — the row is causally load-bearing (`operand_write.py`)

Build `d(A→B)` = diff-of-means of the OBJECT-token residual (built at the object position,
NOT the readout, to dodge the unembed-alignment confound); STEER a recall cloze by adding
`d` at layer L; read logit(A)/logit(B). Clean recall 0.99.

Steering **flips the composed output A→B at flip-rate 1.00 at L2/L7/L13/L20** (+12–14 logit
margin), **0.75 at L26**; matched-random direction ~0 (flip 0.00–0.02); B-specific (+5.6–6.5
over a bystander). **Anti-triviality PASSES**: the effect is strongest MID-STACK (injected
upstream, propagates), not late-only — a genuine operand-row rewrite, not an unembed/
logit-lens nudge. This is the **OPPOSITE of the s250 C-field** (92% decodable yet
causally-inert readout register): the operand row is readable AND writeable.

### 3. HARDENED — dose-responsive + composed + cross-task (`operand_harden.py`)

Sweep the dose α on a **COMPOSED** readout (few-shot category map `operand→its category`:
dog→animal, car→vehicle, rose→plant; clean 1.0; a semantic transform, not a copy), with the
=== fn_index keys ===
11:this file). Keys = mean last-token residual over 3 HELD-OUT exemplar
12:prompts per map minus the grand mean across maps (the "about to apply f"
13:state, map-level, item-independent). Dispatch cell: operand d_E installed
62:        means = {m: np.mean(raw_keys[m][li], axis=0) for m in ALL_MAPS}
63:        gm = np.mean(list(means.values()), axis=0)
65:            keys[(m, li)] = means[m] - gm
```

### Thinking

I'm distinguishing between two approaches: the in-context method that uses hooks to inject operand values and function keys at specific positions during inference, versus the weight-serialized approach that bakes these modifications directly into the model's parameters.

The key challenge is whether baking can capture the operand rebinding that fails in-context—specifically, whether the second hop can condition on the first hop's output. I'm considering whether to bake the function as a slot keyed on the first operation's product register, so that when the first hop executes natively, its output automatically triggers the second hop through the baked weights rather than relying on generic injection.

The distinction matters: if I bake a true function (country-to-capital mapping that works for any country), the slot's key must fire on the country class itself, and the payload must adapt based on which specific country appears. But there's a subtlety around whether the baked payload can actually be operand-dependent or if it collapses to a fixed lookup. The country→capital map is actually resident in the model already — it knows the capitals. What's missing is the in-context linker connecting g's output to h's operand slot. So instead of baking the function itself, I should bake the wire that connects them. But that's still abstract — let me think through what "baking the stack into a delta plate" actually does mechanically. In the in-context version, the operand gets installed at a nonce slot and keys are injected for both g and h, but the problem is h fires generically without its operand being bound to g's product.

The baked version elevates those transient keys into permanent weights across two slots: one slot applies the country-of operation to the installed landmark, and the second slot — keyed on the country-class signature that g produces — applies the country-to-capital mapping. By making the second slot conditional on the first slot's output, baking creates the physical linker wire: h only fires after g has written a country to the register. The test is whether this two-slot baked model can land the composed capital in a single forward pass, winning the argmax and moving errors out of the operand domain.

I'm setting up the control experiments now. The null cases isolate what's doing the work: a direct landmark-to-capital lookup (operand-independent), slot h keyed on the nonce instead of g's product (which should reproduce the in-context failure since h fires without the conditioning), and baselines with shuffled keys and random slots. The crucial comparison is keyed-on-product versus keyed-on-nonce — that difference reveals whether the operand-rebinding wire is what enables composition.

The diagnostic classifier from stack_error_domain.py becomes the readout tool: if baked composition pushes the argmax out of the operand/city domain and into capitals, that's the success signal. But this is a dear experiment, so I need to draft the pre-registration and get Michael's approval before freezing or running anything.

I'm writing the P-BAKE-STACK pre-reg draft directly to the program-plates page under a new section, marked as "status: designing / NOT FROZEN" so it's visible for review. The page edit itself is fine—only commits to mementum need approval—so I'll present the draft with the key design forks flagged and wait for Michael's sign-off before proceeding.

The main decisions Michael needs to make are which slots to bake (I'm leaning toward two keyed slots with the linker isolation as the load-bearing control) and whether the country-to-capital transform can be baked as a function or if it has to stay resident in the routing layer, since we can't INSERT a join directly but the capital map is already resident and ceiling-gated.

There's also a model tension: the composition seam only really works at 32B scale (where 4B takes shortcuts), but the bake machinery was validated at 4B, so we need to decide which host to target. Given how intricate this is and that it's the critical next step, I should draft the pre-registration with these forks laid out explicitly and let Michael decide rather than pushing forward without clarity. I'll present all three forks clearly with a recommendation and ask Michael to decide, then move into drafting the pre-reg section itself.

### Thinking

I'm drafting the §P-BAKE-STACK pre-registration section for the program-plates page, marking it as designing rather than frozen, and grounding it in the diagnostic findings while leveraging the existing operand-bake machinery. I'll insert it after the P-STACK-1b results and am working through the key design claim about how weight-baking installs the operand-rebinding linker wire that in-context injection couldn't achieve.

The diagnostic data from stack_error_domain.py shows the primary success signal: in-context errors clustered heavily in the operand/city domain (83–100%) with nearly zero errors in the stopped-at-g or wrong-capital categories, so P-BAKE-STACK succeeds when baked errors shift out of that operand/city domain and the composed capital wins the argmax. Now I'm identifying the two candidate baked slots at the install layer—one keyed on the resident country-class product direction (fires when a country occupies the product register) and another keyed on the nonce content signature as injected by fn_stack, with the control difference being whether the key depends on the product or remains nonce-agnostic.

The real question is whether the linker wire itself is what needs baking: if the product-keyed slot composes while the nonce-keyed slot fails, then operand rebinding is the physical mechanism. But the capital map is already resident in the model (ceiling-gated), so the honest minimal bake isn't to bake the capital function itself—it's to bake the linker as a routing nudge that keys on the product and directs the country into the resident capital-lookup, which is really just inserting a row that the resident join composes. I'm realizing that g itself isn't running natively; it's an injection within fn_stack keyed on a country-of landmark nonce, so the whole framing needs to account for that.

The cleanest test would be to bake slot_h with the product as the key and the country-to-capital advance as the payload, then run the same fn_stack cell under three conditions: baked-h-keyed-on-product (linker installed), in-context h-injection (the P-STACK-1b regime), and baked-h-keyed-on-nonce (permanent in-context). If baked-h-keyed-on-product moves errors out of the operand and city domain, that's the signal that weight-baking actually conditions hop-2 on hop-1.

Rather than keep iterating in chat, I should write up a draft with a recommended design plus explicit forks and present it to Michael. I'm laying out three approaches: baking just the linker with slot_h keyed on g's product (the minimal, honest test that directly checks if operand rebinding becomes physical), or baking both functions as slots for a fuller program plate, or other variations.

For the base model question, the composition seam shows up at 32B but the baking machinery itself is only validated at 4B, so I'm weighing whether to test at 32B to match the seam despite the unvalidated machinery, or stick with 4B where the tools work even if it means accepting the shortcut confound.

On the hook versus weight question, the keyed-product hook from operand_insert is cheaper than weight serialization, so I should test whether keying the operand on the product actually fixes the collapse before moving to the heavier weight approach. only fires when g's product (country) appears in the residual, confirming the linker hypothesis cheaply before committing to weight baking. Then rung 3b moves to the expensive part: serializing that product-keyed slot with operand baking, quantization, and mirroring to ship the delta. The stack_error_domain diagnostic reads out both rungs, and I've got a solid design now—time to draft the pre-registration with the claim, success signal, the cheap-before-dear ladder, gates, nulls, verdicts, registers, and forks for Michael to review.

**The cheap diagnostic paid off big.** It didn't just answer "conditioning failure vs h-not-firing" — it handed P-BAKE-STACK its **primary success signal**. In-context stack errors are 83–100% operand-domain collapse (h can't rebind g's product); ~0% stopped-at-g; ~0% wrong-capital. So the test becomes concrete and reuses the diagnostic 1:1: **baking passes ⟺ errors move OUT of the operand/city domain (composed capital wins).**

**The design's load-bearing contrast** isolates the wire cleanly: two baked h-slots differing *only* in their key —
- `slot_h·PRODUCT` — keyed on g's **output** (country-class direction) → hop-2 conditioned on hop-1 → *should* compose.
- `slot_h·NONCE` — keyed on the nonce (the in-context regime made permanent) → *should* reproduce the collapse.

Their difference **is** the operand-rebinding wire `product(g) ∈ key_passband(h)`.

**I structured it cheap-before-dear** (λ, and it respects the recursion-antecedent discipline that froze ffn-function-bake before running):
- **3a — product-keyed HOOK** (no weight write): does conditioning h on g's product fix the operand collapse *at all*? This may answer the science question by itself.
- **3b — WEIGHT-SERIALIZE** (only if 3a fires): graduate via the proven `operand_bake → operand_quant → operand_mirror` arc → the shippable ternary delta plate = the level-3 seam-artifact's composition unit.

3. **Depth:** let **3a gate 3b** — stop after 3a if the hook answers it; spend 3b only if the level-3 deliverable needs the shippable plate now.

### Thinking

Now I'm moving on to task #2 and starting task #3, which involves building the 3a instrument. This extends the fn_stack.py framework with a product-keyed hook mechanism where the injection gain for h scales with how much the running residual projects onto the country-class product direction, effectively making h fire in response to g's output.

To implement this, I need to compute the country-class direction as the mean residual when a country token occupies the product register, then at h's window, modulate the key injection by a gain factor based on the projection onto this direction. This conditions the second hop on whether the first hop actually produced a country versus just a nonce or operand. I'll also set up a control condition using the original fixed-window injection to compare the error profiles between product-keyed and nonce-keyed regimes.

The key question is whether g's injected country actually appears in the residual at the final token position where h operates — that's what determines whether h can detect and key on it. For the experiment, I'll extract the country-class direction from exemplars of country tokens in neutral contexts, then use that to gate h's activation based on how much country-content is present in the residual. So the country-class direction is just the mean of all the individual country product directions, normalized to unit length. Then I compute a gain by taking the dot product of the residual at that position with this direction, apply ReLU to keep it positive, and scale it. I multiply the key by this gain before adding it. The tricky part is implementing this as a custom hook that can read the current residual, compute the projection, and conditionally add the scaled key — I'll need to design this carefully in the exploration script.

For the actual instrument, I'm building a product-keyed hook that reuses the existing chain and operand machinery, with two branches: a baseline fixed add versus a gain-modulated add based on the country-class projection. I'll set up gates to compare their performance — checking whether the product version beats the baseline, whether it fires when it should, and whether ablating the gain mechanism breaks it — then validate the whole setup on test worlds.

This is substantial work that requires careful integration with the fn_stack internals, so I'm going to build it directly rather than delegate, especially since I need to verify correctness against the runtime and the diagnostic phase is already done.

```
def stack_verdict(gate0: bool, sc: dict, g2_flip: bool) -> str:
    if not gate0:
        return "negative/inconclusive (gate-0)"
    g1 = sc["compose_vs_parts"].verdict
    g_rand = sc["vs_random"].verdict
    if not (g1 and g2_flip and g_rand):
```

### Thinking

Now I'm moving into building bake_stack.py for the 3a configuration, which requires careful engineering work. I need to construct a country-class product direction component, implement a custom gain-modulated hook that captures the residual at the final position and projects it onto the country-class dimension while applying a key-based gain multiplier, and set up multiple experimental arms including a baseline, gain-only variant, a fixed stack configuration, a gain-modulated stack variant, and another gain-only branch.

The full pipeline includes readout logic with error-domain classification reusing existing stack error domain patterns, gate verdicts G1-G4, and validation across planted worlds at both 4B and 32B model scales. The hook mechanics are the critical piece here—reading the residual inside the forward pass, constructing the country-class direction properly, and normalizing the gain correctly are all load-bearing for correctness, so I'm going to build this directly rather than delegate it, since the mechanical details of what "product-keyed" means live in the diagnostic work I've already done.

For the country-class direction, I need to capture it at the h-window layer where country-product forms emerge. The simplest honest approach is to build it from the mean last-token residual at layer w_h over prompts that produce a country token (like "The landmark is located in the country of {country}"), subtracting out the grand mean versus cities to isolate country-ness at the readout. When the gain hook fires at w_h at the final token position, it reads the residual there and projects onto this direction, assuming the injected signal from w_g has propagated the country-product information to that final position.

The real question is whether the injected country signal actually materializes as country-content at the final token by w_h. The earlier test found this was weak — the product-key gain conditions on whatever country-ness actually emerged, so if little emerged, the gain stays quiet (which is correct behavior, since the diagnostic showed the country never wins). This is exactly what test 3a is designed to measure: the gain function honestly conditions on measured country-ness, firing only if that content is present rather than firing blindly like the nonce arm would.

I'm defining the gain function as a normalized ratio of the final residual's projection onto the country direction, clipped between 0 and a cap around 1.5, calibrated against a reference scale from prompts where country is actually present. Then the key contribution scales with this gain, so the nonce arm (which always has gain ≡ 1) serves as a baseline for comparison. I'm setting up the experimental arms: baseline with no keys, g-key alone to see if it produces country content, and the stack with nonce to test the full mechanism.

Now I'm laying out the remaining arms to test different hypotheses—the stack with gain modulation to see if conditioning on country-class works, an ablated version without the g-key to check if h alone can fire on country, and a null case to isolate the fact-form contribution. The key insight is that the nonce arm and ablated arm give me the control conditions I need, while the product arms test whether the gain mechanism actually gates the behavior. I'll keep 3a focused on the LINKER isolation gates—comparing error domains between PRODUCT and NONCE, checking composed accuracy, and ablating conditioning effects—with G4 deferred to 3b. The 3a verdict becomes a binary LINKER-FIRES or LINKER-FAILS that gates whether 3b proceeds, and I'm defining each gate precisely using statistical tests like McNemar and permutation tests over the cell data.

Now I'm setting up validation by simulating planted worlds with argmax categories and gains to exercise the verdict logic, then writing the implementation using the existing stack imports. I'll also need the classify logic from stack_error_domain. Given the scope and complexity here—building, validating, and running smoke tests with specific acceptance criteria—I could delegate this to a subagent, but the gain mechanics are subtle enough that I should build it directly to get them right. Let me write bake_stack.py now, though I need to gather a few more details first.

I'm checking the remaining parts of fn_stack for the constants and configuration I'll need—the nonce candidates, depth values, pairs, key exemplars, and how the arguments are structured.

```
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="P-STACK-1 in-context composition")
    ap.add_argument("--validate", action="store_true")
    ap.add_argument("--chain", default="continent",
                    choices=["continent", "capital"],
                    help="continent = frozen P-STACK-1; capital = §P-STACK-1b")
    ap.add_argument("--model-id", default="Qwen/Qwen3-4B")
    ap.add_argument("--device", default="mps")
    ap.add_argument("--dtype", default="bfloat16", choices=["float32", "bfloat16"])
    ap.add_argument("--ref-layer", type=int, default=9)
    ap.add_argument("--scale", type=float, default=2.0)
    ap.add_argument("--key-scale", type=float, default=2.0)
    ap.add_argument("--n-cells", type=int, default=0,
                    help="cap landmarks (0 = all valid; smoke uses ~8)")
    ap.add_argument("--alpha", type=float, default=0.05)
    ap.add_argument("--seed", type=int, default=0)
    ap.add_argument("--out", default="results/fn-stack/qwen3-4b")
    args = ap.parse_args()
    if args.validate:
        return run_validate(args.alpha)
    return run_model(args)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(main())
```

```
FRAMES = [
    "The travelers admired {x}.",
    "A postcard showed {x}.",
    "The documentary featured {x}.",
    "The guidebook described {x}.",
    "Tourists photographed {x}.",
    "The lecture mentioned {x}.",
    "A painting depicted {x}.",
    "The article discussed {x}.",
]


def first_tid(tok, w):
    return tok(" " + w, add_special_tokens=False).input_ids[0]


def cap_hook(store, li):
    def hook(_m, _i, out):
        h = out[0] if isinstance(out, tuple) else out
        store[li] = h.detach().float().cpu().numpy()
    return hook


def add_hook_at(vec_t, pos):
    def hook(_m, _i, out):
        h = out[0] if isinstance(out, tuple) else out
        if 0 <= pos < h.shape[1]:
            h[0, pos, :] = h[0, pos, :] + vec_t.to(h.dtype)
        return out
    return hook


def resolve_parts(model):
    """(decoder-layers, final-norm, lm_head) across architectures (dense + hybrid)."""
    inner = model.model
    lm = inner if hasattr(inner, "layers") else inner.language_model
    return lm.layers, lm.norm, model.lm_head


def main() -> None:
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("--model-id", default="Qwen/Qwen3-4B")
    ap.add_argument("--ref-layer", type=int, default=9)          # install layer
    ap.add_argument("--scale", type=float, default=2.0)
    ap.add_argument("--swap-layers", type=int, nargs="+", default=[11, 15, 20])
```

### Thinking

Now I'm building `bake_stack.py` and need to carefully structure it by pulling in the necessary constants and imports from the other modules—reusing things like the country mappings and exemplars from `fn_stack`, the key exemplars from `fn_index`, the nonce candidates from `holo_cap`, and utility functions like the permutation logic and error handling from the other dependencies. I need to set up the key data structures: landmark directions at the reference layer, country keys at specific layers, a new country-class product direction built by averaging product directions across test countries at the target layer and unit-normalizing, and a calibration metric that measures mean projection of country-present residuals onto this direction to normalize gain. Then I'm implementing a forward hook on the decoder at that layer which reads the final token's residual, projects it onto the direction, clips the projection relative to the calibration baseline, and injects the key.

The hook adds after the layer's output, so the earlier country key has already propagated through by this point. The direction itself captures country content at the final token position at the target layer, which is the right measurement since the injected key forms its effect there in subsequent layers. Now I need to establish the calibration reference projection for the gain computation. I'll use a calibration set of prompts where the country token appears naturally at the end — something like "... the country of {country}" — and measure the projection onto the country direction at layer w_h for those cases. That gives me a reference baseline for what "full country-ness" actually looks like in the model's activations, then I can compare the g-alone arm against it. I'll keep the projection consistent by measuring the reference value the same way I use it at runtime — just projecting raw residuals onto the direction without subtracting the baseline, since the grand mean stays roughly constant across the fixed prompt. The nonce-only baseline should produce a much smaller projection than the country-present reference, which is the whole mechanism. Let me code this up and see if the validation and smoke tests confirm the numbers work as expected.

I'm going to write bake_stack.py in one pass with the full structure: imports and constants, the classify function, the scoring logic that computes gates from the arms and gains, verdict function, validation runner for planted worlds, model runner, and main entry point. This is a substantial file but I need to get the calibration right before testing with the 4B model.

Writing bake_stack.py... Operand-domain errors are those where the argmax falls into CITY or COUNTRY categories but isn't marked CORRECT. I'm setting up three key gates: G1 checks if the product has fewer operand-domain errors than the nonce variant, G2 verifies the product achieves better accuracy than all baselines, and G3 ensures the gablate variant shows near-zero accuracy with substantially lower gain values, indicating successful conditioning. I need to track per-cell gains for stack_product and gablate, then validate the planted worlds by simulating cells with domain outcomes across both arms to verify the verdict. For the reference layer in computing gain and d_cc, I'll use the h-window layer from whichever pair is being tested—the pre-registration allows sweeping the same pairs as fn_stack, but I need to build d_cc at each distinct h-layer present in those pairs. This is getting complex, so I'll keep the smoke test focused on the frozen best pair region for now.

np.mean(list(means.values()), axis=0)\n        for m in key_specs:\n            keys[(m, li)] = means[m] - gm\n    key_norms = {m: float(np.linalg.norm(keys[(m, key_layers[0])])) for m in key_specs}\n    print(f\"[bake3a] key norms @L{key_layers[0]}: \"\n          f\"{ {m: round(v, 1) for m, v in key_norms.items()} }\")\n\n    # ── country-class product direction d_cc @ each h-layer + gain calibration ─\n    #    d_cc = unit(mean_country residual − mean_city residual) at the h-layer;\n    #    ref_proj = typical country-present projection (the gain=1 reference).\n    h_layers = sorted({lh for (_, lh) in pair_layers})\n    d_cc, ref_proj = {}, {}\n    city_calib = [mh3.CITY_OF[lm] for lm in mh3.LM_LIST]\n    for lh in h_layers:\n        c_res = [capture_hidden(CC_FRAME.format(x=c), [lh])[lh] for c in CC_CALIB]\n        city_res = [capture_hidden(\"The traveler visited {x}\".format(x=ct), [lh])[lh]\n                    for ct in city_calib]\n        cc_raw = np.mean(c_res, axis=0) - np.mean(city_res, axis=0)\n        u = cc_raw / (np.linalg.norm(cc_raw) + 1e-9)\n        d_cc[lh] = u\n        # reference = mean projection of country-present residuals (raw, minus city mean)\n        proj = [float(np.dot(r - np.mean(city_res, axis=0), u)) for r in c_res]\n        ref_proj[lh] = max(float(np.mean(proj)), 1e-6)\n    print(f\"[bake3a] d_cc ref_proj: { {lh: round(v, 2) for lh, v in ref_proj.items()} }\")\n\n    # ── hooks ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n    def gain_hook(vec_t, lh, city_mu_t):\n        \"\"\"Add vec_t at the FINAL token scaled by country-ness gain (product-keyed).\"\"\"\n        u_t = torch.tensor(d_cc[lh], dtype=torch.float32, device=dev)\n\n        def hook(_m, _i, out):\n            h = out[0] if isinstance(out, tuple) else out\n            last = h.shape[1] - 1\n            r = h[0, last, :].detach().float() - city_mu_t\n            proj = float(torch.dot(r, u_t).item())\n            gain = float(np.clip(proj / ref_proj[lh], 0.0, args.gain_cap))\n            h[0, last, :] = h[0, last, :] + (vec_t * gain).to(h.dtype)\n            hook.gain = gain\n            return out\n        hook.gain = 0.0\n        return hook\n\n    city_mu = {lh: torch.tensor(\n        np.mean([capture_hidden(\"The traveler visited {x}\".format(x=ct), [lh])[lh]\n                 for ct in city_calib], axis=0), dtype=torch.float32, device=dev)\n        for lh in h_layers}\n\n    def cell_logits(lm, adds, gains=None):\n        \"\"\"adds: list of (layer, vec, mode) mode in {'fixed','gain'}. Returns logits, gain.\"\"\"\n        prompt = NONCE_PROMPT.format(x=nonce)\n        ids = tok(prompt, return_tensors=\"pt\").to(dev)\n        toks = ids.input_ids[0].tolist()\n        occ = [i for i, t in enumerate(toks) if t == nonce_tid][-1]\n        handles, gain_hooks = [], []\n        vt = torch.tensor(d_lm[lm] * S, dtype=torch.float32, device=dev)\n        handles.append(dec[L].register_forward_hook(mh3.add_hook_at(vt, occ)))\n        for (li, vec, mode) in adds:\n            kt = torch.tensor(vec * args.key_scale, dtype=torch.float32, device=dev)\n            if mode == \"gain\":\n                hk = gain_hook(kt, li, city_mu[li])\n                handles.append(dec[li].register_forward_hook(hk))\n                gain_hooks.append(hk)\n            else:\n                handles.append(dec[li].register_forward_hook(mh3.add_hook_at(kt, len(toks) - 1)))\n        with torch.no_grad():\n            lo = model(**ids).logits[0, -1, :].float().cpu().numpy()\n        g = float(np.mean([hk.gain for hk in gain_hooks])) if gain_hooks else 0.0\n        for hd in handles:\n            hd.remove()\n        return lo, g\n\n    def argmax_word(lo):\n        return max(union, key=lambda w: lo[union[w]])\n\n    cells = [lm for lm in valid if target_of(lm) in union]\n    cats = build_categories([{\"country\": mh3.COUNTRY_OF[lm], \"city\": shortcut_of(lm)}\n                             for lm in cells])\n    print(f\"[bake3a] cells: {len(cells)}\")\n\n    per_pair, records = {}, []\n    for (lg, lh) in pair_layers:\n        kg, kh = keys[(\"country\", lg)], keys[(\"country2cap\", lh)]\n        rows = []\n        for lm in cells:\n            truth = target_of(lm)\n            lo_base, _ = cell_logits(lm, [])\n            lo_g, _ = cell_logits(lm, [(lg, kg, \"fixed\")])\n            lo_n, _ = cell_logits(lm, [(lg, kg, \"fixed\"), (lh, kh, \"fixed\")])\n            lo_p, gain_s = cell_logits(lm, [(lg, kg, \"fixed\"), (lh, kh, \"gain\")])\n            lo_ab, gain_ab = cell_logits(lm, [(lh, kh, \"gain\")])   # g ablated\n            row = {\"landmark\": lm, \"truth\": truth, \"country\": mh3.COUNTRY_OF[lm],\n                   \"city\": shortcut_of(lm),\n                   \"base_arg\": argmax_word(lo_base), \"galone_arg\": argmax_word(lo_g),\n                   \"nonce_arg\": argmax_word(lo_n), \"product_arg\": argmax_word(lo_p),\n                   \"gablate_arg\": argmax_word(lo_ab),\n                   \"gain_stack\": gain_s, \"gain_gablate\": gain_ab, \"pair\": [lg, lh]}\n            rows.append(row)\n            records.append(row)\n\n        def dom(a, lm_truth):\n            return classify(a, lm_truth, cats)\n\n        def acc(argkey):\n            return float(np.mean([1.0 if dom(r[argkey], r[\"truth\"]) == \"CORRECT\"\n                                  else 0.0 for r in rows]))\n\n        def operr(argkey):\n            return np.array([1.0 if (dom(r[argkey], r[\"truth\"]) in OPERAND_DOMAIN)\n                             else 0.0 for r in rows])\n\n        sc = score_3a(operr(\"nonce_arg\"), operr(\"product_arg\"),\n                      acc(\"product_arg\"), acc(\"nonce_arg\"), acc(\"base_arg\"),\n                      acc(\"galone_arg\"), acc(\"gablate_arg\"),\n                      np.array([r[\"gain_stack\"] for r in rows]),\n                      np.array([r[\"gain_gablate\"] for r in rows]), rng, alpha)\n        v = verdict_3a(gate0, sc)\n        per_pair[f\"{lg}-{lh}\"] = {\n            \"rebinding\": asdict(sc[\"rebinding\"]), \"g2_flip\": sc[\"g2_flip\"],\n            \"g3_conditioning\": sc[\"g3_conditioning\"], \"acc\": sc[\"acc\"],\n            \"operand_frac\": sc[\"operand_frac\"], \"gain\": sc[\"gain\"], \"verdict\": v,\n            \"_rb\": sc[\"rebinding\"].value}\n        print(f\"[bake3a] L{lg}->L{lh}: rebind Δop={sc['rebinding'].value:+.3f} \"\n              f\"(p={sc['rebinding'].p:.4f}) acc_prod={sc['acc']['product']:.2f} \"\n              f\"acc_nonce={sc['acc']['nonce']:.2f} g3={sc['g3_conditioning']} \"\n              f\"gain s/ab={sc['gain']['stack']:.2f}/{sc['gain']['gablate']:.2f} -> {v}\")\n\n    best_pair = max(per_pair, key=lambda k: per_pair[k][\"_rb\"])\n    verdict = per_pair[best_pair][\"verdict\"]\n    for k in per_pair:\n        del per_pair[k][\"_rb\"]\n    print(f\"[bake3a] best pair {best_pair} -> VERDICT: {verdict}\")\n\n    result = {\n        \"model_id\": args.model_id, \"stage\": \"3a-product-keyed-hook\",\n        \"seed\": args.seed, \"scale\": S, \"key_scale\": args.key_scale,\n        \"gain_cap\": args.gain_cap, \"ref_layer\": L, \"n_layers\": n_layers,\n k, v in ref_proj.items()},\n        \"n_cells\": len(cells), \"gate0\": gate0, \"per_pair\": per_pair,\n        \"best_pair\": best_pair, \"verdict\": verdict, \"cells\": records}\n    out = Path(args.out)\n    out.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    (out / \"bake_stack.json\").write_text(\n        json.dumps(_json_safe(result), indent=2, allow_nan=False))\n    print(f\"[bake3a] wrote {out}/bake_stack.json\")\n    return 0\n\n\ndef main() -> int:\n    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=\"P-BAKE-STACK 3a product-keyed hook\")\n    ap.add_argument(\"--validate\",     def gain_hook(vec_t, lh, city_mu_t):

### Thinking

The 4B smoke test ran successfully end-to-end with the instrument working and gate0 passing all checks, though the linker failed at 4B. What's striking is that the gain values are essentially identical whether g's product is included or ablated—the stacked and g-ablated arms show nearly matching gains across the board, suggesting that g's injection isn't actually conditioning the country-ness projection as expected.

This aligns with what we'd predict from the P-STACK-1b diagnostic: at 4B, g doesn't cleanly write a country product due to operand-domain collapse, so the gain hook can't condition on something that isn't measurable. However, there's another possibility worth considering—the instrument itself might be the issue. The landmark direction installed at L9 already carries strong country association, so by L22 the residual might project onto country-ness regardless of whether the g-key is added, making the d_cc / ref_proj measurement insensitive to g's actual contribution.

The real test is at 32B where g and h should be more separable and g's product more distinct. Before running that, I need to verify whether the gain hook can discriminate at all—whether it's actually capable of detecting g's contribution or if there's a fundamental calibration problem. The operand's latent country association creates a confound—the country-CLASS direction fires regardless of whether g explicitly produced the country or the operand just implies it. For the gain to genuinely measure g's conditioning effect, it needs to isolate the country product appearing specifically due to g, but the operand's inherent country-ness contaminates the signal. This suggests the 3a design might have a fundamental confound built in.

That's exactly what the g-ablation control is designed to catch: if the gain stays roughly the same when g is ablated, then the gain isn't actually conditioned on g, and the linker fails to properly bind. The 4B results show this—the product-key gain isn't conditioned on g because the operand's latent country-ness dominates, so the linker fails. The real test is whether 32B can separate these effects.

Running 32B will take longer (maybe 15-40 minutes given the complexity), but it's the decisive experiment. Before committing to that, though, I'm noticing a potential design issue: the operand's latent country-ness could contaminate the gain at any scale. At 32B specifically, the model becomes more strictly typed, which might actually prevent the operand from projecting onto country-ness without g's key—that could be the key difference that makes the linker work at scale.

The 4B result fits the expected 4B→32B pattern and doesn't rule out success at 32B. I should run it as planned, but I need Michael's approval first since the established protocol is to run 32B jobs through his tmux session. The g-ablation control already captures what I need to measure, so there's no additional fix required there. Now I'm checking the smoke JSON output to make sure the per-cell data looks reasonable and the classification didn't produce any artifacts. Let me verify the reference projection values and gain ranges are sensible at the different layers.

### Thinking

The 32B model shows the same pattern as 4B: it fails to link across all four test pairs, and the gain from ablating g is nearly identical to the stack gain (around 0.30-0.37 across the pairs). This means the country-class gain isn't actually dependent on g's key information—the product-key activates at roughly the same level whether g is present or not.

This is a genuine finding, not just noise from a weak model. The original hypothesis predicted that at 32B, g's key injection would create measurable country information that the gain keys would latch onto, while removing g would drop the gain significantly. But the data shows gain_stack ≈ gain_gablate at both scales, which suggests either the operand itself (the landmark at the nonce slot) already carries latent country associations that d_cc picks up on, or g's key doesn't actually produce additional country-ness by the time it reaches the final token in the h-layer. Both interpretations point to the same problem: the country-class direction d_cc is contaminated by the operand's own latent country signal, so the gain can't cleanly isolate g's contribution.

Looking at the per-cell breakdown, gablate (operand plus h-gain-hook, no g) shows nearly identical country-ness to stack (operand plus g plus h-gain-hook), which means g is adding almost nothing. This could be an instrument limitation, but it's actually revealing something real: the product-keyed-hook approach as currently designed can't install the linker because the conditioning signal at readout is dominated by the operand's intrinsic associations rather than by g's explicit product. In the λ verbum frame, the operand's key already carries its type and associations; g's "product" isn't a distinct, addressable country-token at the final position that h can actually key on.

The diagnostic data shows that errors never land on COUNTRY (0% stopped-at-g), which means g's country product isn't cleanly written to the final readout position at all. There's no addressable country product for h to rebind to — the intermediate country exists during g's computation but isn't materialized in the residual as something h can read. This is sharper than just operand contamination: the in-context linker can't be installed via residual-projection gain because the intermediate product lacks an addressable form.

But this creates a tension with the weight-baking plan. If g's product isn't addressable in-context, can baking actually install the wire? The 3b approach would bake a slot keyed on the country-class product, but if that product isn't materialized in the injected case, the question becomes whether the native model (running g without injection) actually materializes the country in its depth-flow. The native 3-hop does compose landmark→country→continent in depth, so natively the country product exists — the issue is that injecting the key at a window doesn't reproduce that native flow.

So the honest finding: injected g-keys don't produce an addressable country product for h to rebind — this is a null result for the product-keyed-hook approach over injected keys. But this doesn't necessarily kill weight-baking of the composition itself; it just means the in-context injection substrate is too weak to produce the intermediate. The real composition happens natively in depth. Let me check the per-cell readouts and gains more carefully to verify this.

The key diagnostic is whether the gains are both high (operand saturates country-ness) or both low (neither produces much). Looking at the numbers, gains are around 0.3, meaning the operand gives modest country-ness but g adds nothing on top. Now I need to examine the per-cell distribution to see if there's an argmax pattern and how g-alone behaves at 32B.
