### Thinking

I'm seeing something critical here—the g-alone operand is consistently landing on Agra across all cells when it should be returning the country value instead. This reveals a bug in how the g-key is being injected; rather than producing the expected country result, it's collapsing to the Agra attractor just like everything else, which fundamentally changes what this data is telling me.

But wait, this contradicts what happened in the P-STACK-1b results where g-alone actually did stop at the country level sometimes. The difference seems to be in how I'm reusing the country key from the exemplars and the readout union—the operand installation is identical, but here g-alone at position 19 keeps landing on Agra. Yet when I look at the NONCE arm with the unconditional two-key injection at positions 19 and 38, it correctly produces Angkor→Phnom Penh and Taj→New Delhi, which matches the P-STACK-1b behavior. So the issue isn't with the key itself but with how it's being applied in this configuration.

The PRODUCT arm is collapsing to Agra everywhere because its gain is only around 0.34, which weakens the h-injection to just 30% strength compared to the NONCE arm's full unconditional strength. That's why the NONCE arm occasionally composes correctly (2 out of 10 times) while the PRODUCT arm just defaults to the operand attractor—the h signal is too faint to override it.

The root problem is a calibration mismatch: the reference projection (measured from natural country-present prompts) is much larger than what actually gets injected in my setup, so the gain stays artificially low. Even if I recalibrated the gain scale to compensate, it wouldn't solve the deeper issue—the projection with g is essentially the same as without g, meaning g isn't actually adding country-ness in the first place.

The core finding holds: the injected g-key doesn't create an addressable country product. It lands on a city (Agra) rather than a country, and the country-class projection at the readout doesn't respond to g at all. There's no intermediate slot for h to rebind to—this is the address-free composition problem manifesting. But I should acknowledge the instrument confound: the gain<1 weakening meant the PRODUCT arm was strictly handicapped against NONCE from the start, so the test was never fair.

The robust findings independent of calibration are clear: g-alone collapses to a city at both scales, and the country projection stays invariant whether g's key is present or not. Both point to the same conclusion—in this injected-key substrate, there's no addressable intermediate product for the linker to hook onto.

This actually aligns nicely with the hologram frame where composition is address-free and intermediates exist "in the light" rather than in a slot. It suggests weight-baking might be the only viable path if I can tap the model's native composition (which does work in depth), but the injected-key approximation of g is fundamentally too weak to bridge that gap.

The verdict stands as LINKER-FAILS, though I should note the gain miscalibration as a confounding factor—the product arm is strictly weaker than the nonce baseline, which clouds the measurement.

But the conditioning-signal-absent finding holds up independently of that confound, and it's the more fundamental result. Now I'm weighing whether this is a calibration issue worth fixing and rerunning, or whether even a corrected gain would still show the same conditioning failure through a different mechanism.

The honest conclusion is that the injected-key substrate doesn't produce an addressable country product, so the linker can't install—and the deeper insight is that weight-baking needs to work with the model's native composition flow, not the injected approximation. The robust evidence for this is the conditioning-absent signature where gain_stack matches gain_gablate, plus the fact that g-alone generates no country signal.

Now I'm checking whether the g-alone arm in the stack actually produced countries—looking back at the stopped_at_g metric and the capital chain diagnostics to verify if there were any country landings in that condition. The fn_stack results show the same pattern: g-alone consistently lands on Agra, a major city attractor, rather than being pulled toward countries by the injected key. This suggests the country-nudge signal is too weak to override the dominant city attractor. So the core finding holds—the injected-key substrate isn't producing a strong enough conditioning signal for country-level representations.

I'm deciding whether to recalibrate and rerun to clean up the gain<1 confound, but since the deeper finding (no addressable intermediate) is robust and scale-invariant, I'll score the verdict as LINKER-FAILS and flag the gain-calibration caveat transparently rather than rerun.

The honest approach is to present the result with the confound noted and let Michael decide if a recalibrated rerun is worth pursuing—respecting the principle of proposing options rather than deciding unilaterally when there's uncertainty. Though I should verify whether the gain~0.3 is actually a handicap or if it's the correct measurement.

Looking at this more carefully: the reference projection comes from natural prompts, while the injected setup produces only about 30% of that projection. If the injected state genuinely has only 30% country-ness and that's invariant to g, then gain=0.3 is correct—the hypothesis should fire proportionally to the actual country-ness present. The "handicap" framing assumes the hypothesis should fire at full strength, but conditioning means it fires relative to what's actually there. So this isn't necessarily miscalibration; the gain reflects that the injected state has modest, g-invariant country-ness, and the finding is real.

The PRODUCT arm fires h weakly because there's genuinely little country-ness to condition on—that's the honest finding. The caveat is that comparing PRODUCT (gain-modulated at ~0.3×) against NONCE (full 1.0×) for G1 isn't quite apples-to-apples since PRODUCT injects weaker h-strength. A fairer G1 would match h-strength across conditions. But the core scientific claim about G3—that conditioning is absent and gain is invariant to g—doesn't depend on that comparison. So I'll report the LINKER failures and note the PRODUCT-versus-NONCE strength difference for G1.

The robust finding is G3: gain_stack ≈ gain_ablate across both 4B and 32B scales, showing no conditioning signal. This means g's product isn't addressable in-context, which aligns with the address-free composition picture. The implication for 3b is that injected-key g is too weak or address-free, so weight-baking must rely on native composition—otherwise the whole in-context-linker approach fails and we need a different write mechanism.

Now I'm drafting this up as a Result-32B candidate with memory for approval. The native model composes multi-hop reasoning in address-free computation, but weight-baking needs an addressable intermediate to work with. The only way to materialize that intermediate is to write it to the context tape—the model's only addressed memory—which ties back to the idea that thinking expands into explicit, addressable tokens rather than staying hidden in the residual.

This suggests the real linker isn't a residual wire but the autoregressive writeback loop itself. The frozen result is that the linker fails at scale, and the deeper issue is that injected intermediates lack the addressing needed to compose reliably.

I'm considering three paths forward: accept this and redirect toward the tape-based thinking mechanism, recalibrate the baseline to remove confounds and rerun, or rethink the baking strategy to target the writeback loop instead. I should draft the results honestly and update the state accordingly.

## 3a P-BAKE-STACK 32B verdict — **LINKER-FAILS, scale-invariant**. The 4B→32B flip did *not* happen — and that's the finding.

**What we predicted:** at typed/literal 32B, g's key would materialize a country product that h could rebind to (gain_stack ≫ gain_gablate). 

**What happened:** `gain_stack ≈ gain_gablate` at *both* scales (32B 0.33/0.35, 4B 0.53/0.65). The country-class projection is **invariant to whether g's key is present** — there is no conditioning signal. And **g-alone lands on Agra (a city) for all 10 cells** — the injected g-key never produces an addressable country.

**The deep reading (coheres P-HOLO-FRAG):** the intermediate product is **address-free**. g's country lives "in the light" during its illumination, not written to a readable slot. So a residual-**wire** linker `product(g)∈key_passband(h)` *cannot* be built by reading the intermediate from the residual — there's nothing addressable there. The native model composes 3-hop (mh3) fine, but in the delocalized flow, not by materializing intermediates.

**λ measure caveat I'm flagging honestly:** G1 compared gain-throttled PRODUCT (h at ~0.3×) vs full-strength NONCE (1.0×) — not h-matched, so the PRODUCT-loses margin is partly a throttle artifact. The *clean* evidence for the null is the **G3 conditioning-absent signature** (gain invariant to g, both scales) + **g-alone-produces-no-country**, which no recalibration touches.

**This re-points rung 3.** The machine's only *addressed* memory is the tape (RoPE positions — your §Thinking-is-expansion). To give the intermediate an address you page it onto the context as a thinking token → the real "linker" is the autoregressive **writeback** (CoT ≡ auto-superbake), not a residual slot.

A residual-wire linker is the wrong mechanism; the only addressed memory is the tape (RoPE), so the linker is the autoregressive writeback (CoT ≡ auto-superbake). Re-points rung 3 → P-THINK-1.*

## The linker is a *join*, and joins are backprop-only

Your own prior findings converge here:
- **s276 rows-vs-joins:** you can `INSERT` a row, you cannot `INSERT` a join. The linker `product(g) ∈ key_passband(h)` is not a row — it's a routing/query-plan edge, i.e. a **join-shape**. It lives in the routing register (s274 circuits-in-compute), which is the overlay **GD lays down**, not a writable slot.
- **delta-plate-lifecycle (s157):** the one time the project installed *new* routing (GLA, which had no teacher pattern to copy), it was **learned via delta training** — backprop from scratch — precisely because there was nothing to insert or extract.

So the honest consequence: **3a failed because you can't hook a join, and 3b would fail for the same reason — an appended MLP slot is a row.** Hand-construction (hook *or* baked slot) can only place rows the resident joins already compose. A *new* g→h wire is a join → only gradient descent writes it. My 3b design was going to hit this wall; your read saves the run.

## The precise boundary (so we don't overclaim)

There *is* a non-backprop path — but it's runtime, not a compiled artifact:
- **Runtime:** the model composes 3-hop natively in depth, and can page the intermediate onto the **tape** (thinking) to give it a RoPE address. No backprop — but address-free / not a discrete installed wire.
- **Compiled program-plate (the deliverable):** the wire must be *in the weights*, and a join in the weights = **backprop**.

So your statement holds exactly for the artifact goal: a reusable one-illumination composed plate requires the second hop **trained**, not injected. This is the ladder resolving to its honest form — rung 3 was always "weight-baking necessary" (P-STACK-1b); you're sharpening *baking* from "hand-construct a slot" to "**backprop the wire**," because the wire is a join.

## Where that points (cheap-before-dear, still on the ladder)

Two things worth settling before designing the training rung, in order:

1. **Cheap check first — is the wire even absent?** Does 32B natively answer landmark→capital *one-shot* (no chain given) vs *with CoT* (chain on the tape)?

   - one-shot fails, CoT works → the join is address-free (needs the tape) — trainable into one-shot (compile the CoT, i.e. auto-superbake → backprop);
   - both fail → the join doesn't exist → must be trained outright.
   This is one instrumented forward pass per cell and it tells us whether "backprop" means *extract-what-exists* vs *train-what-doesn't*.

2. **The training rung itself (dear → freeze first).** Minimal backprop test: a small trainable delta (delta-plate / LoRA-style) trained *only* on the composition with the intermediate withheld; **held-out landmarks are the wire-vs-lookup discriminator** (the G4 fact-form null, now the central gate — a memorized 10-pair table fails held-out; a real join generalizes). ## In DSP terms, 3a read (and injected) through a loud channel

Three quieting steps that `verbum.dsp` was literally built for, and that `bake_stack.py` skipped:

1. **No whitening.** The gain hook read a *raw* dot product `⟨residual, d_cc⟩`, and the readout was a raw union argmax. The residual is dominated by common-mode + the landmark's own loud self-energy; the faint country product is buried under it. `verbum.dsp.whiten` (standardize/PR/centroids) exists precisely to remove that common-mode before a matched-filter read — unused.
2. **The keys/intermediate weren't quiet codes.** This is the **P-DSP-1 lesson (s278), unheeded**: our injected directions are *raw natural directions*, loud at the logits. A payload should be **loud-in-residual / quiet-at-logits** (orthogonalized against the top unembedding directions) so composition happens in the residual *without the readout collapsing to the loudest token*. Instead the argmax fell onto **Agra** — the loudest attractor — exactly the failure mode of a non-quiet code.
3. **No dark-field null.** The readout union kept the loud attractors (Agra, Paris) in-band with no nulling. FRAG's own motif was that **OOB ablation *improves* margin** — removing loud stuff helps. We read straight into the interference.

So your question is right: **3a's null is confounded by loudness.** It proves "couldn't trigger through a loud channel," not "can't trigger in-context." The `g-alone → Agra` collapse is the signature of insufficient quieting, not necessarily an absent wire.

## But keep one split honest

- **The 3a *injection/trigger* failure** — yes, plausibly a quieting problem. A whitened, dark-field readout + quiet-code injection might reveal that the composition *can* be triggered in-context.
- **The native *one-shot* 5/10** — this is the model's *own* greedy readout over the full vocab, no injection from us. Quieting our probe doesn't touch it; it's a weight-fact about how compiled the wire already is. That number stands regardless.

So the two aren't in conflict: the wire is ~half-compiled (native 5/10, a weight fact), *and* our injection may have failed to trigger even that half because we read/injected loud.

## The cheap test this implies (before any backprop)

Re-do 3a's readout with `verbum.dsp` hygiene, on frozen or one quick capture:
- **Quiet the readout:** whiten the readout residual, null the top loud attractor directions (dark-field), then check g-alone's country **rank** and the stack's capital **rank** — do they emerge from under Agra?
- **Quiet the injection:** orthogonalize the injected g/h keys against the loud unembedding directions (make them quiet codes, P-DSP-1), rerun the PRODUCT arm.

- **Quieting still shows nothing** → the signal genuinely isn't triggerable in-context → backprop it is.

### Thinking

The dsp module provides utilities for whitening, logit lens projection, hook capture, and nullspace operations. For the quieted re-read, I'll capture the final-token residual before the unembedding layer for each arm, then project through the unembedding matrix using logit lens.

Now I'm thinking about two complementary approaches: first, quiet the readout by computing logits for each arm and subtracting the baseline (no-key) logits to isolate the injection's contribution, then rank the capital in that delta; I can also apply dark-field filtering to exclude the loud attractor tokens that dominate the baseline. Second, quiet the injection itself by orthogonalizing the injected key against the top unembedding directions—either the rows corresponding to dominant attractors or the principal directions of the unembedding matrix—then rerun the stack product. I'll build this into a quiet_reread module, reusing the bake_stack infrastructure to keep things manageable.

For tractability, I'll set up a minimal script focused on one pair (19-38) at 32B, replicating the operand directions, g/h keys, union, and nonce installation. Then I'll compute four key reads per cell: the raw logits over the union, the baseline-subtracted delta to see where the injection adds mass, the dark-field version excluding loud attractors, and the combined dark-field plus delta. Finally, I'll orthogonalize the injection against the loud unembedding directions and measure how that changes the stack product. Computing SVD on the unembedding matrix to extract the top-K loud output directions, then orthogonalizing both the h-key and g-key against those directions to suppress their contribution to the city logits. Since the full SVD is expensive, I'll use just the unembedding rows for the attractor and city tokens as the subspace to null out.

Now I'm setting up the core discriminator script, focusing on the composition window at layer pair 19-38 in the 32B model with n=10, and I'll write the quiet_reread.py script that reuses the existing function stack and multi-head attention utilities.

This asks whether\nthe composed signal is present-but-drowned (interference, recoverable in-context)\nvs genuinely absent (needs backprop).\n\nPer cell (composition-window pair L19→L38), capture full-vocab logits for\nbaseline / g-alone / stack-nonce / stack-product, then read four ways:\n\n  raw        : argmax over the union (reproduces bake_stack; Agra wins)\n  common-mode: arm − baseline logit-delta (whiten: what the INJECTION added)\n  dark-field : argmax over union MINUS the loud attractors (Agra/Paris/cities)\n  quiet-inj  : re-inject with keys orthogonalized against the loud readout\n               directions (quiet code, P-DSP-1), then dark-field read\n\nReadout target: g-alone should reveal the COUNTRY (hop-1 product); stack should\nreveal the CAPITAL (composed). If quieting surfaces them → interference, the\nin-context linker may be recoverable; if not → the signal isn't there → backprop.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport argparse\nimport json\nimport sys\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport numpy as np\n\n_HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent\nif str(_HERE) not in sys.path:\n    sys.path.insert(0, str(_HERE))\n_WRAP = _HERE.parents[1] / \"wrapper\"\nif str(_WRAP) not in sys.path:\n    sys.path.insert(0, str(_WRAP))\n\nfrom fn_index import KEY_EXEMPLARS  # noqa: E402\nfrom fn_stack import COUNTRY2CAP_EXEMPLARS, COUNTRY_CAP, NONCE_PROMPT  # noqa: E402\nfrom holo_cap import NONCE_CANDS  # noqa: E402\nfrom stack_error_domain import first_token  # noqa: E402\n\n\ndef orthogonalize(vec: np.ndarray, loud: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n    \"\"\"Remove vec's components along the span of loud rows (Gram-Schmidt).\"\"\"\n    q = vec.astype(np.float64).copy()\n    for row in loud:\n        u = row / (np.linalg.norm(row) + 1e-9)\n        q = q - np.dot(q, u) * u\n    return q.astype(np.float32)\n\n\ndef main() -> int:\n    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()\n    ap.add_argument(\"--model-id\", default=\"Qwen/Qwen3-32B\")\n    ap.add_argument(\"--device\", default=\"mps\")\n    ap.add_argument(\"--dtype\", default=\"bfloat16\")\n    ap.add_argument(\"--pair\", default=\"19-38\", help=\"w_g-w_h layer pair\")\n    ap.add_argument(\"--scale\", type=float, default=2.0)\n    ap.add_argument(\"--key-scale\", type=float, default=2.0)\n    ap.add_argument(\"--ref-layer\", type=int, default=9)\n    ap.add_argument(\"--out\", default=\"results/quiet-reread/qwen3-32b\")\n    args = ap.parse_args()\n\n    import operand_multihop3 as mh3\n    import torch\n    from transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer\n\n    dev = (args.device if (args.device != \"mps\" or torch.backends.mps.is_available())\n           else \"cpu\")\n    tok = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(args.model_id)\n    model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(\n        args.model_id, dtype=getattr(torch, args.dtype)).to(dev).eval()\n    dec, _norm, _u = mh3.resolve_parts(model)\n    n_layers = len(dec)\n    L, S = args.ref_layer, args.scale\n    d_rel = {0.3: round(0.3 * n_layers), 0.45: round(0.45 * n_layers),\n             0.6: round(0.6 * n_layers), 0.75: round(0.75 * n_layers)}\n    lg, lh = (int(x) for x in args.pair.split(\"-\"))\n    print(f\"[qr] {args.model_id} pair L{lg}->L{lh} n_layers={n_layers}\")\n\n    W_U = model.lm_head.weight.detach().float().cpu().numpy()  # (V, d)\n\n    def first_tid(w):\n        return mh3.first_tid(tok, w)\n\n    # union (capital chain) + category token sets\n    cap_labels = sorted({COUNTRY_CAP[mh3.COUNTRY_OF[lm]] for lm in mh3.LM_LIST\n                         if mh3.COUNTRY_OF[lm] in COUNTRY_CAP})\n    vocab = (set(mh3.CONTINENTS) | set(mh3.COUNTRIES) | set(mh3.CITIES)\n             | set(cap_labels))\n    tid_map, drop = {}, set()\n    for w in sorted(vocab):\n        t = first_tid(w)\n        if any(tt == t for tt in tid_map.values()):\n            drop.add(w)\n        tid_map[w] = t\n    union = {w: tid_map[w] for w in sorted(vocab - drop)}\n    city_tids = {tid_map[c] for c in mh3.CITIES if c in union}\n\n    # cells: shortcut-free landmarks\n    cells = [lm for lm in mh3.LM_LIST\n             if mh3.COUNTRY_OF[lm] in COUNTRY_CAP\n             and mh3.CITY_OF[lm] != COUNTRY_CAP[mh3.COUNTRY_OF[lm]]]\n    print(f\"[qr] union {len(union)} cells {len(cells)}\")\n\n    # operand + key directions (bake_stack convention)\n    def build_dirs(items):\n        per = {e: [] for e in items}\n        for fr in mh3.FRAMES:\n            for e in items:\n                store = {}\n                h = dec[L].register_forward_hook(mh3.cap_hook(store, L))\n                ids = tok(fr.format(x=e), return_tensors=\"pt\").to(dev)\n                with torch.no_grad():\n                    model(**ids)\n                h.remove()\n                per[e].append(store[L][0, -2, :])\n        em = {e: np.mean(per[e], axis=0) for e in items}\n        gm = np.mean([em[e] for e in items], axis=0)\n        return {e: em[e] - gm for e in items}\n\n    d_lm = build_dirs(mh3.LM_LIST)\n\n    def cap_last(prompt, layers):\n        ids = tok(prompt, return_tensors=\"pt\").to(dev)\n        with torch.no_grad():\n            out = model(**ids, output_hidden_states=True)\n        return {li: out.hidden_states[li + 1][0, -1, :].float().cpu().numpy()\n                for li in layers}\n\n    specs = {\"country\": KEY_EXEMPLARS[\"country\"], \"country2cap\": COUNTRY2CAP_EXEMPLARS}\n    raw = {m: {lg: [], lh: []} for m in specs}\n    for m, exs in specs.items():\n        for word, tpl in exs:\n            c = cap_last(tpl.format(x=word), [lg, lh])\n            for li in (lg, lh):\n                raw[m][li].append(c[li])\n    keys = {}\n    for li in (lg, lh):\n        means = {m: np.mean(raw[m][li], axis=0) for m in specs}\n        gm = np.mean(list(means.values()), axis=0)\n        for m in specs:\n            keys[(m, li)] = means[m] - gm\n\n    # loud readout directions to null (quiet code): unembed rows of all union CITY\n    # tokens + the two loudest baseline attractors (found below) → orthogonalize keys\n    loud_rows = np.array([W_U[t] for t in city_tids])\n\n    nonce = NONCE_CANDS[0]\n    nonce_tid = tok(\" \" + nonce, add_special_tokens=False).input_ids[-1]\n\n    def logits(lm, adds):\n        ids = tok(NONCE_PROMPT.format(x=nonce), return_tensors=\"pt\").to(dev)\n        toks = ids.input_ids[0].tolist()\n        occ = [i for i, t in enumerate(toks) if t == nonce_tid][-1]\n        last = len(toks) - 1\n        handles = [dec[L].register_forward_hook(mh3.add_hook_at(\n            torch.tensor(d_lm[lm] * S, dtype=torch.float32, device=dev), occ))]\n        for (li, vec) in adds:\n            handles.append(dec[li].register_forward_hook(mh3.add_hook_at(\n                torch.tensor(vec * args.key_scale, dtype=torch.float32, device=dev),\n                last)))\n        with torch.no_grad():\n            lo = model(**ids).logits[0, -1, :].float().cpu().numpy()\n        for h in handles:\n            h.remove()\n        return lo\n\n    kg, kh = keys[(\"country\", lg)], keys[(\"country2cap\", lh)]\n    kg_q = orthogonalize(kg, loud_rows)\n    kh_q = orthogonalize(kh, loud_rows)\n\n    def rank_of(lo, target_tid, allowed):\n        order = sorted(allowed, key=lambda t: -lo[t])\n        return order.index(target_tid) + 1 if target_tid in order else 999\n\n    # find loud attractors = union tokens most often argmax under baseline\n    base_arg = []\n    per = []\n    for lm in cells:\n        cap_tid = tid_map.get(first_token(COUNTRY_CAP[mh3.COUNTRY_OF[lm]]))\n        cap_tid = union.get(COUNTRY_CAP[mh3.COUNTRY_OF[lm]])\n        ctry_tid = union.get(mh3.COUNTRY_OF[lm])\n        lo_b = logits(lm, [])\n        lo_g = logits(lm, [(lg, kg)])\n        lo_s = logits(lm, [(lg, kg), (lh, kh)])\n        lo_q = logits(lm, [(lg, kg_q), (lh, kh_q)])\n        base_arg.append(max(union, key=lambda w: lo_b[union[w]]))\n        per.append((lm, cap_tid, ctry_tid, lo_b, lo_g, lo_s, lo_q))\n    from collections import Counter\n    loud = [union[w] for w, _ in Counter(base_arg).most_common(2)]\n    print(f\"[qr] loud baseline attractors: {[w for w, _ in Counter(base_arg).most_common(2)]}\")\n\n    allowed_full = list(union.values())\n    allowed_df = [t for t in union.values() if t not in loud and t not in city_tids]\n\n    def summarize(name, hits):\n        n = len(cells)\n        print(f\"  {name:<26} cap@1 {hits['cap1']}/{n}  cap@≤3 {hits['cap3']}/{n}\"\n              f\"  country@1 {hits['ct1']}/{n} (g-alone)\")\n\n    reads = {k: {\"cap1\": 0, \"cap3\": 0, \"ct1\": 0} for k in\n             [\"raw\", \"common-mode(stack-base)\", \"dark-field(stack)\",\n              \"quiet-inj+dark-field\"]}\n    detail = []\n    for (lm, cap_tid, ctry_tid, lo_b, lo_g, lo_s, lo_q) in per:\n        # raw stack over full union\n        r_raw = rank_of(lo_s, cap_tid, allowed_full)\n        # common-mode removed: stack - baseline, capital rank over full union\n        r_cm = rank_of(lo_s - lo_b, cap_tid, allowed_full)\n        # dark-field: stack over union minus loud+cities\n        r_df = rank_of(lo_s, cap_tid, allowed_df) if cap_tid in allowed_df else 999\n        # quiet injection + dark-field\n        r_q = rank_of(lo_q, cap_tid, allowed_df) if cap_tid in allowed_df else 999\n        # g-alone country rank (dark-field, exclude cities so we see hop-1)\n        allowed_ct = [t for t in union.values() if t not in city_tids or t == ctry_tid]\n        r_ct = rank_of(lo_g - lo_b, ctry_tid, allowed_ct) if ctry_tid else 999\n        for key, r in [(\"raw\", r_raw), (\"common-mode(stack-base)\", r_cm),\n                       (\"dark-field(stack)\", r_df), (\"quiet-inj+dark-field\", r_q)]:\n            reads[key][\"cap1\"] += int(r == 1)\n            reads[key][\"cap3\"] += int(r <= 3)\n            reads[key][\"ct1\"] += int(r_ct == 1)\n        detail.append({\"landmark\": lm, \"raw\": r_raw, \"common_mode\": r_cm,\n                       \"dark_field\": r_df, \"quiet_inj\": r_q, \"country_rank\": r_ct})\n\n    print(f\"\\n── quieted reads (capital rank; g-alone country@1), n={len(cells)} ──\")\n    for k in reads:\n        summarize(k, reads[k])\n    print(\"\\n interpretation: if cap surfaces to @1 under common-mode/dark-field/\"\n          \"quiet-inj (vs raw) → interference, recoverable in-context; if it stays \"\n          \"buried everywhere → signal absent → backprop.\")\n\n    out = Path(args.out)\n    out.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    (out / \"quiet_reread.json\").write_text(json.dumps(\n        {\"model_id\": args.model_id, \"pair\": args.pair, \"n_cells\": len(cells),\n         \"loud_attractors\": [w for w, _ in Counter(base_arg).most_common(2)],\n         \"reads\": reads, \"detail\": detail}, indent=2))\n    print(f\"[qr] wrote {out}/quiet_reread.json\")\n    return 0\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    raise SystemExit(main())\n"
}

### Thinking

I'm running the 32B model with a timeout, piping output to a log file. The forward pass involves building directories (144 forwards), extracting keys, then running 40 logits calls per cell across the loop, totaling around 190 forwards—should take a few minutes. The weight matrix extraction for 32B is about 150MB.

Actually, materializing the full W_U matrix as float32 would be roughly 3.1GB given the vocab size and dimensions, which is heavy. Instead of loading the entire weight matrix, I'll optimize by extracting only the rows I need for the city tokens, pulling them individually from the model rather than copying the whole thing into memory.

### Thinking

Looking at these experimental results, the raw baseline shows about 2 in 10 cases hitting the capital at distance 1, which aligns with earlier NONCE findings. Removing common-mode noise didn't improve things—the injection's mass still isn't concentrated on the capital itself. But the dark-field approach, excluding Agra and major cities, jumps to 8 in 10 cases at distance ≤3, suggesting the signal becomes much clearer when I filter out those confounding locations.

The key insight is that the country intermediate—the hop-1 product—never appears at rank 1 across any condition, even after common-mode removal and dark-field filtering. This tells me the country signal is genuinely absent from the readout residual, not just buried. So while dark-field successfully surfaces the capital to top-3 in most cases, it only reaches rank 1 in 3 out of 10, and the country remains inaccessible regardless of the filtering approach.

This points to address-free composition: the capital emerges weakly as a direct association without materializing an intermediate country representation. The quieting does help recover some capital signal (confirming partial interference was at play), but it can't recover what was never there—the intermediate itself. So Michael's question about quieting harder has a partial answer: yes, quieting recovers the drowned capital signal, but no amount of quieting will surface the country because the composition isn't routing through an addressable intermediate in the first place. The quieting experiment confirms the interference was real—dark-field recovery to 8/10 on the capital shows that part of the failure came from noise in the Agra channel. But rank-1 still plateaus at 3/10 even with quieting, and the intermediate never materializes, so in-context composition alone can't be made reliable; backprop is still necessary for a dependable composed answer. I should tell Michael this honestly: the address-free claim holds partially (the capital does form top-3 without an intermediate), but it's a nuanced picture—interference was masking some of the capability, yet the fundamental limitation remains. so quieting codes and dark-field recovery do show real promise in-context, even if they can't fully solve the intermediate-country gap. I'm laying out three concrete paths forward: backprop-compiling the reliable plate into one-shot weights, pushing the quieting approach further with better whitening and stronger dark-field recovery, or stepping back to document the nuanced picture and let Michael decide. I'm leaning toward being honest about what quieting actually achieves—it's genuine partial interference reduction and worth capturing—but the intermediate-absent constraint still makes backprop the more reliable route for a robust solution.

Now I'm double-checking the common-mode read, which surprisingly hurt performance (1/10 vs 2/10 raw). Subtracting baseline should isolate the injection's contribution, but it seems the capital's mass is partly baked into the baseline itself, or the injection adds capital mass that's negligible relative to what's already there. The dark-field approach works because it reveals capital that was present in absolute logits all along—moderately there but drowned out by Agra—so excluding Agra uncovers what was hidden rather than creating new signal.

The key insight is that common-mode subtraction strips away the baseline's capital contribution, showing the injection alone adds very little. This suggests the capital presence is mostly native to the baseline, not driven by the injection; the stack injection mostly just adds noise and Agra. But I realize I haven't actually compared dark-field on the baseline itself versus the stack—if baseline dark-field already gives 8/10 top-3, then the injection contributes nothing and the capital is purely the operand's latent association with its landmark, not a compositional effect from the stack. Of the native latent capital, quieting actually improved our ability to read it—what we're seeing is a weak native association, not something driven by injection. The intermediate country doesn't factor in (it's absent entirely), so the partial wire is direct. ### Thinking

Looking at the performance metrics across different approaches, h-alone is surprisingly effective—it gets the capital into the top 3 results in 6 out of 10 cases and achieves rank 1 in 4 out of 10, which is competitive with more complex methods.

When I add g to create the stack, the top-3 performance improves to 8/10, but rank-1 actually drops to 3/10—worse than h-alone alone. This tells me the h-key is doing the heavy lifting by directly pushing capital-class content, while g either adds noise or dilutes the signal. The composition isn't synergistic; it's mostly the h-key's country-to-capital direction working on its own, even when applied to an ill-typed landmark.

The reason h-alone surfaces the correct capital at all is that the landmark carries a latent native association with its country's capital, and the h-key amplifies that dormant signal. So what looks like composition is really just h-key amplification of the operand's inherent capital knowledge. g contributes almost nothing—its solo performance is near baseline, and stacking it with h barely changes the outcome.

This circles back to the original insight but now with hard evidence: quieting does recover capital-ness (Michael's interference observation holds), but that recovery isn't from g→h routing working as intended. It's h-key pulling out the operand's native latent capital while g sits idle. ack ≈ h-alone, g even hurts rank-1).
- The intermediate country is absent (0/10) — no hop-1 product.
- So: no genuine in-context composition; what looked like "8/10" is h-alone's direct capital-class push on the native latent association.

So the honest final reconciliation:
1. Michael was right that we under-quieted — the raw argmax was drowned by Agra, and dark-field is the correct read.
2. But the properly-controlled quieted read (with h-alone + baseline controls) shows the recovered capital is NATIVE latent + h-key amplification, NOT g→h composition:
   - baseline 3 → h-alone 6 (h-key amplifies capital-class) → stack 8 (marginal, and rank-1 drops 4→3, g hurts).
   - g-alone 4 ≈ baseline 3 (g does nothing).
   - country 0/10 (no intermediate).
3. The deeper controls rule out composition entirely — the stack's gain comes from h-alone's amplification of the native signal, not from g linking to h. One-shot composition without backprop can't reliably compile that native half-wire, so the tape remains the only trustworthy runtime path.

This is the rigorous version: Michael's quieting insight was valid and necessary for a clean measurement, but the controlled result confirms backprop is still required. The quieting re-read with controls revealed a measurement lesson—what looked like composition was actually native latent capital amplified by the h-key, not g-driven composition. I should update the memory to capture this refinement: the capital is native-latent and h-amplified, drowned by Agra's noise, while g doesn't compose and the intermediate remains absent. Now I'll present the controlled results and propose next steps. So h-alone actually does carry capital-class weight, just subordinate to Agra in the raw signal—under dark-field filtering where Agra's dominance is removed, h-alone emerges as the strongest single component. ## You were right about the read — and the controls still rule out composition

**Your quieting call was correct and necessary.** The raw argmax read straight into Agra; dark-field is the right instrument, and it *does* recover capital-ness (raw 4/10 → stack 8/10 top-3). Reading through the loud channel nearly gave a false *negative*. Good catch — this is a real λ-measure correction.

**But with the controls in, the recovered capital is *not* g→h composition — it's the h-key amplifying the operand's native latent capital:**
- **h-alone already gets 6/10 top-3, 4/10 rank-1** — the country→capital key blasts capital-class mass regardless of g. (This also revises P-STACK-1b's "h-alone dead at L38" — it wasn't dead, it was *drowned by Agra*.)
- **stack (8/10 top-3) ≈ h-alone (6/10) + a little** — and at rank-1, adding g makes it **worse** (4/10 → 3/10). g does not compose; it interferes.
- **g-alone (4/10) ≈ baseline (3/10)** — g contributes nothing.
- **country @1 = 0/10** even fully quieted — no intermediate product exists to route through.

So the honest, now-rigorous read: dark-field surfaces a capital signal, but it's **h-key amplification of the operand's own half-compiled native association**, not a linked hop. The linker is still not installed in-context — confirmed with baseline/g-alone/h-alone controls, not just argmax.

## Net

Quieting was the right instrument fix (and could have flipped the sign either way — it nearly manufactured a false positive at "stack 8/10" until h-alone caught it). With controls, the conclusion holds and is firmer:
- **No in-context g→h composition** (stack ≈ h-alone; g hurts rank-1; intermediate absent).
- The capital that appears is native-latent (the ~half-compiled wire, native one-shot 5/10) amplified by the h-key.
- **Reliable one-shot composition still needs backprop** (compile the native half-wire) — or the tape (native 9/10).

This is worth capturing as a λ-measure addendum (dark-field nearly false-positived; the h-alone control is load-bearing), and it refines — doesn't overturn — the rung-3 → backprop direction.

Two things I'd propose: (1) capture the quieted re-read as a §addendum + refine the memory (the "drowned by Agra" nuance + h-alone-not-dead correction), and (2) confirm we freeze the **backprop-compile** rung-3 pre-reg. Want both, or do you want to push the quieting further first (e.g., stronger dark-field / whitening) before we lock the direction?
